Tests: `SpaceRevampBugListTests` (6, 10, 13, 15, 16, 18, 20), `ShipAccessRulesTests` (22), the mining and salvage tests (1-3), `CombatUpgradeBibleRecipeParityTests` (15).

**Deferred items, triaged by the gaps pass (W11j):**

- **Closed in W11j:**
  - Mining and strip mining give Gathering XP (`ShipSalvageActions.GiveGatheringExperience`, the gas and salvage rule: the asteroid tier x 10 against the Gathering rank), not Piloting XP. Test: `GatheringSpaceIndustryTests`.
  - A droid at a station runs an E-War array (`DroidCrewTask.ElectronicWarfare`): on while an enemy ship is within the array's range, off once none is (`DroidCrewRules.FlipsElectronicWarfare`, `Space.UpdateDroidElectronicWarfare`). The array's accuracy penalty now scales by the station effectiveness of whoever runs it, as damage and repairs do, so a droid jams at its effectiveness and a player crew member at up to 105%. Test: `SpaceDroidCrewTests`.
  - Bug 15: the Bible's `Missile25` row (above).
  - `crew_console`: no Large or Capital interior lacks one. Every Large and Capital hull uses the Corvette, Merchant or Throne layout, and every converted capital deed (`capdeed_*`) had the Corvette layout; old Merchant and Throne deeds became Small and Medium ships, which have no crew seats.
  - Cloak over a restart and while docked: settled by the shipped rules. A logout ends the cloak and entering space clears every toggle, so no cloak outlives a restart; a docked ship is out of space (its cloak ends on docking) and no host can fit a Cloaking Device (hosts are Large or Capital). W11n added an explicit docked refusal and a login cleanup of a crash-saved cloak effect.
  - Generators: `stations.py` now writes W11g's station exterior (it overwrote it), and `droids.py --check` ignores CRLF line ends as `catalog.py` does.
- **Decided and built in W11k** (see "Added by W11k" below): Espionage XP for scanning, the player Interdictor, Ion Web, Jammer, Target Painter and Afterburner modules (so `IsShipInterdicted` is real), rejoining a Rift Run after logging out, and a Hull Traits row on the Ship HUD.
- **Closed in W11k without a build:**
  - A droid running the Data Analyzer: built in W11n. It has no mini-game; it opens the pilot's targeted data site by chance equal to its station effectiveness (a miss loses the target, as a player's failure does) (see Droid crew, Behavior).
  - The industrial parts on Equipment - Droids: they stay off that tab (it would need a new Industry column) and are listed on the Industrial Droids (Revamp) tab only.
- **Owner decisions (open):**
  - None left. Dangerous-lane marking was decided and built in W11n (below).
- **Decided in W11n** (see "Added by the seat-skill update (W11n)" below): the Engineering and Espionage rank bonuses (section 4) and the Espionage bonus to the cloak reveal range (sections 6 and 32).
**Added by W11k (the W11j owner decisions, decided and built):**

- **Ion Web, Interdictor, Jammer, Target Painter and Afterburner** (plan Appendix B; the Ship Modules (Revamp) tab's draft rows, unchanged):
  - **Families:** `ShipModuleFamily` 47-51 and `ShipModuleType` 27-31 (`IonWeb`, `Interdictor`, `Jammer`, `TargetPainter`, `Afterburner`), Mid row, Piloting, toggles, Mk I-X at ranks 0-45 (`sm_web_*`, `sm_intd_*`, `sm_jam_*`, `sm_paint_*`, `sm_aburn_*`, Module/uti; needs a module repack). `catalog.py` adds them as `EWAR_LATE_FAMILIES` after the Cargo Expander, so no earlier value moves: recipes `RecipeType` 7743-7792 (the highest in use was 7742), each its tier's Sensor Suite (the Afterburner a Thruster Assembly) and refined ore, recipe level max(2, rank), cost 100 + 30 x rank. Crafted only, as the Cloaking Device and Cargo Expander are: the dockhand store's list is unchanged and no loot table drops them.
  - **Numbers** (draft rows; cycle s / capacitor / range): Ion Web 3 / 3 / 20 m, target speed -(20 + rank / 3)% (Mk I 20%, Mk X 35%); Interdictor 4 / 5, range 15 + rank / 5 m (15-24 m, a per-rank stat `InterdictRange` as the Probe Launcher's sweep range is, so the family keeps one `Range`); Jammer 4 / 4 / 30 m, target Accuracy -(4 + 0.4 x rank) (-4 to -22); Target Painter 4 / 4 / 30 m, target damage taken +(5 + rank / 9)% (5-10%); Afterburner 2 / 3, +(25 + rank / 3)% speed (25-40%). The draft gives the Jammer and Target Painter no range; they take the E-War Array's 30 m. No draft number was changed.
  - **Hull bonuses** (`ShipElectronicWarfareTuning`, from the Ships (Revamp) tab): the Interceptor role's "Ion Web and Interdictor strength +25% and range +50%" (web Mk X 44%, web 30 m, hold 36 m; the Interdictor has no strength number), the Republic Scout's "Ion Web range +20%" (adds to the role's: 34 m) and the Barracuda's "Immune to Ion Webs" ("That ship's hull shrugs off Ion Webs."). A web is capped at 75% slow (the Storm Cannon's), which no hull reaches.
  - **Behaviour** (`SpaceElectronicWarfare.cs`, `ShipElectronicWarfareRules`, `ShipAfterburnerRules`): the four hostile modules follow the ship's target like weapons (`ShipToggleRules.TargetsEnemy`: PvP rules gate them as weapons, and a droid at their seat runs them at the pilot's target, `DroidCrewTask.Weapon`), need another ship ("Target an enemy ship.") and renew their effect every cycle for the cycle + 0.5 s, so it lapses a moment after the module stops. They are not weapons: power routing, All Weapons and the decloak lock leave them alone. A web (tagged `EffectMovementSpeedDecrease`), jam or paint works at the runner's station effectiveness, as the E-War Array does. A jam shares the E-War Array's Accuracy penalty and paints share one field: the stronger one counts, so stacking adds nothing. The paint adds to the target's damage taken in `RollWeaponShot`, so every weapon and ability shot on it does more. Each cycle puts the firing ship on the target's enmity table and credits a support combat point, as the E-War Array does.
  - **Interdictor** (`Space.IsShipInterdicted`, now real; `ShipStatus.InterdictedUntil`): while held, the target cannot jump a starlane (`PlaceableScripts.UseTeleportDevice`), dock at a starport or station (`StarportDockDialog`, checked on opening and on docking) or aboard a ship (`DockWithTarget`), board a derelict, open a Rift Run (`GetRiftKeyRefusal`), start a Jump to Signature or cross a rift (`BeginSignatureJump`; the first cycle of a hold breaks off a spooling jump with "An interdiction field holds your ship. The jump fails."; `CompleteSignatureJump` already refused a held ship), or cloak (`CloakError`, so a cloak in progress also ends within a cycle). Each refusal says "An Interdictor holds your ship."; the first cycle of a hold tells the pilot "... You cannot jump, dock or cloak while it holds."
  - **Cloak and Afterburner interactions (decision):** a web or a hold breaks a cloak on its target as damage does ("An Ion Web broke your cloak." / "An Interdictor broke your cloak."; a ship still engaging fails, one that vanished decloaks with the weapons lock). A held ship's Afterburner switches off at once ("Afterburner Mk N switched off: An Interdictor holds your ship.") and cannot be switched on while held. Cloaking switches the Afterburner off like every toggle (W16).
  - **Afterburner:** self-centered toggle; while on, `ApplyShipSpeed` multiplies the ship's speed by the strongest running Afterburner (after power routing and the cloak), and switching it off by hand or any auto-off restores the speed (`TurnOffToggle`, `TurnOffShipToggles`). The Ship HUD's Speed line includes it.
  - **Art:** no free ESS9 model, so each item has its own ESS8 inventory model (W11l): Ion Web 522/238, Interdictor 522/239, Jammer 522/240, Target Painter 522/241, Afterburner 522/242. W11k first reused the retired configuration modules' models, which broke the one-model-per-function rule (`ItemIconTests`). Models 238-242 were compatibility aliases that no base 522 or 534 blueprint has ever used (every `Module/uti` version in history), so no saved item carries them; the 64x64 icons render each module's action-icon SVG without its background square. New action icons (illustrated SVG sources in `SWLOR_Haks/sw_item_source/general`, `item-artwork.json`): `iit_smionweb`, `iit_sminterdict`, `iit_smjammer` (Control), `iit_smtgtpaint` (Harmful), `iit_smafterburn` (Self), each with its lit `iit_sl*` icon (`GenerateLitModuleIcons.py`) and recharge frames for both (`GenerateCooldownIcons.ps1`); manifest and `ShipItemIconBindings.csv` rows. No TLK or 2DA rows: modules use the `ife_sm*` placeholder feats, as every module does. Needs a hak build.
  - **Balance** (`checks.py` section P, new; sections A-O unchanged, since no model input changed): a matched Fighter that swaps its Targeting Computer for a Jammer or Target Painter stays within the ground time against every Normal role at every tier (slowest 89% of it) and never beats the Targeting Computer by more than 10% solo (85-105% of its job score): a support pick, not an upgrade. In a 3-Fighter party against a Boss, one Jammer or Painter gives up to 114% of the all-Targeting-Computer party's job (no band; a second one adds nothing). Budget: the jam matches the Targeting Computer's Accuracy step (-22 against +20 at rank 45) and the paint the Damage Amplifier's (+10%, every type). The web, hold and Afterburner change speed and escape only (the simulator has no movement).
  - **Bible:** the Ship Modules (Revamp) tab's five rows carry the built rules (`bible_tabs.py`), the Engineering Recipes tab the 50 recipe rows (`bible_recipes.py`), and the Space Balance tab section P.
- **Espionage XP for scanning:** each signature a Probe Launcher cycle locates (its strength for that scanner crosses 100%, `ScanningRules.Located`) gives `Skill.GetDeltaXP(tier x 10 - Espionage rank)` (`ScanningRules.EspionageXpDelta`, tiers 1-6, the Gathering rule's shape; 0 below the XP table) to the player who runs the launcher: the crew member at the Sensors seat, otherwise the pilot (`SpaceCrew.GetStatSource`); a droid at the seat earns none, and DMs none.
- **Rift Run rejoin:** the run remembers each pilot's ship (`RiftRun.ShipIds`). A pilot who logs out while the run is open leaves their ship waiting in the pocket (`RiftRunRules.MarkAway`, `RiftRun.AwayShips`). Taking the controls again while the run is still open (not cleared and returning, not closing) launches the ship into the current chamber (`GetRiftRunRejoinLocation`, from `TakeShipControls`), and on arrival the pilot is one of the run's ships again (`RiftRunRules.Rejoin`): they fight on, keep their payout share and return where they opened the pocket ("You rejoin the Rift Run in chamber N of 3, the ...: m:ss left."). A closed run keeps the W15b behaviour: the ship goes back to its dock when the pocket closes. A run every pilot left still closes at once (W15b's abandon rule), so a solo pilot's logout ends their run.
- **Ship HUD Hull Traits:** the Stats tab's SHIP section has one Hull Traits row after Power Routing with the hull's trait names (`ShipTraitRules.HullTraitNames`), or "None"; a list longer than the fixed value column (16 characters) moves into the label ("Hull Traits: A, B, C"). Module traits stay in Ship Management.
- **Migration:** none needed. The new `ShipStatus` fields (`InterdictedUntil`, `PaintedPercent`, `PaintedUntil`) read as zero from saved data, the families, types and recipes are new values, and `RiftRun` is in memory only.
- **Tests:** `SpaceElectronicWarfareTests` (catalog rows and numbers, recipes, target modes, droid tasks, hull bonuses, hold, jam and paint timing and stacking, the Afterburner, the built modules, and the wiring of the hold, paint, speed and PvP gates) and `SpaceW11kGapTests` (scan XP, Rift Run rejoin, Hull Traits); `RevampCatalogTests` and `ShipComponentTests` counts and maps updated.
- **Needs client evidence:** each module on, off and auto-off with its lit icon and recharge sweep; a web slowing and a jam, paint and hold on a target (HUD and messages); a held ship refused at a starlane, a dock and a signature jump, its Afterburner switching off and a cloak breaking; the Afterburner's speed on the HUD; Espionage XP on locating a signature; a logout and rejoin in a Rift Run; the Hull Traits row.
- **Content or out of scope (unchanged):** Trellis2 models, station raids and defenses, hostile boarding, lore review, the Capital Industrial role, incursion-specific creatures and capital battle events, more zone aces, standalone mines, pre-placed wrecks, DM derelict, signature, cloak and pocket tools, per-tier pockets, and saving signatures over a restart. (Player-station blockades, new derelict interiors, holdings at starports and outdoor sites, more decor and more achievements were built by W11o; see sections 14, 26, 28 and 30.)

**Added by W11n (dangerous lanes and DM space tools):**

- **Dangerous lanes (decision):** a lane is dangerous for the pilot using it when the system it leads to is a higher tier than the one the pilot is in, is one of the three Small and Medium hull zones, or is Wild Space (`StarlaneDangerRules.Classify`, `StarlaneDanger` flags; tiers from `TradeCatalog.Orbits`, Wild Space 5.5; a system with no tier never counts as higher; no starlane leads into Wild Space today, rifts do, and the rule covers it for any lane added later). When anyone first enters a space area (`Space.MarkDangerousStarlanesOnEnter`, `SpaceStarlanes.cs`), each dangerous lane in it gets a tag on its name (" [Danger: Tier 2, Zone]") and a "Dangerous lane." paragraph in its description saying why (tougher ships, hull limit, other players can attack, and a ship destroyed within 30 seconds of a player's hit, or whose pilot logs out then, loses every module: about 65% drop as loot and the rest are destroyed). Jumping one sends the pilot and the party members who jump with them an orange "Warning: <system> is dangerous." line before the jump (`Space.WarnOfDangerousStarlane`, called from `PlaceableScripts.UseTeleportDevice`). The jump is not blocked: the tag and the warning are the marking. Safe lanes (to the same or a lower tier) are untouched, so most outward lanes now carry a tier tag.
- **DM Tools Space tab:** the tab is two columns in the Placeables tab's 1000 x 470 panel (a 530 px panel cut the Placeables tab off in #3000). Left: Spawn Squad, Start Incursion, Start Convoy, as before. Right (`DmSpaceToolRules`, `SpaceDmTools.cs`, a `Space` partial):
  - **Place Derelict:** click the ground or an object; places a boardable, not contested, derelict signature of the area's tier there and opens its site at once (`RevealSite` with `openToAll`, so the wreck has no party claim). **Place Signature:** the same click places a hidden signature of the picked kind (Combat, Data, Cache, Gas Site or Derelict) for a Probe Launcher to find; a DM-placed cache is never locked. Both work only in the orbits, zones and Wild Space systems that hold signatures (`ScanningTuning.Areas`), since only those areas end and check sites. A placed signature fades or ends like any other and is not counted toward the area's signature count until its refill.
  - **Rift Pockets:** Open Pocket (click a player's ship; opens a pocket of the picked key tier for it and its party with the same checks as a key, but no key is spent: `OpenRiftRun` is the shared opener), Close Pocket (stand inside one; its ships go back where they opened it, the run is set to Closing and the area is removed once nobody, the DM included, is inside) and List Pockets (the count in the tab, one line per run in the DM's chat).
  - **Uncloak Ship:** click a cloaked ship to break its cloak as damage does (`EndCloak`: visible again, weapons locked for a moment).
- **Tests:** `StarlaneDangerTests` (classification, texts, ASCII, the module's real lanes, wiring) and `DmSpaceToolTests` (pickers, refusals, texts, wiring, panel size); `ScanningTests` and `PlayerMessageAudit.json` updated.
- **Needs client evidence:** the lane tags and description on a lane, the warning on a jump, and the DM Tools Space tab layout and each button in a live client. Needs a server deploy only (no module data changed).
- **Not built here:** a confirmation step before a dangerous jump, a DM tool to reveal a cloaked ship to the DM without breaking the cloak, placing a data site, cache or rift by other DM tools, and any change to the Bible.

**Added by W11o (deferred crafting and rewards):**

- **Rare parts in Advanced hull recipes** (sections 7 and 14, Appendix D), held to the W11e hull cost band (no hull above 1.27x its pre-revamp material value: 8 / 12 / 18 / 30 refined ore of its tier plus half that in electronics, Advanced Small 420, Medium 948, Large 1,890, Capital 3,945). The crafted rare components (Thorilide Core 1,163, Aurodium Plating 650, Prototype Core 2,063) cannot fit a Small, Medium or Large hull (533-2,400 in all), so:
  - **Small, Medium and Large Advanced hulls** have a rare-trait slot (section 14's "salvaged parts carrying a rare trait"): one more component of the hull's tier (2 / 3 / 4), of a family its recipe already takes, carrying a rare trait. The slot is not a recipe component: the crafting window fills it (`ShipRareComponentRules.HasRareSlot`, `RareSlotKey`) and the craft fails like a missing component without one. The recipe detail adds "1x Tier 2 component with a rare trait (Hull Frame, Ship Reactor, Thruster Assembly, Energy Emitter)".
  - **Capital Advanced hulls** take their maker's rare component (`components.RARE_BY_MAKER`) in place of the part it is built on and the hull's electronics (`RARE_REPLACES`: Thorilide Core for the Capital Class Reactor, Aurodium Plating for one Capital Class Structural Components, Prototype Core for the Sensor Suite). A tier 5 component of any salvage family with a rare trait stands in for it ("(or, for the Thorilide Core, a tier 5 component with a rare trait)"). Only the Republic Centurion is a Capital Advanced hull today.
  - **Window:** `ShipComponents.IsStandInCandidate` collects qualifying items, `CraftViewModel.AggregateStandIns` spends them on the slot or the rare component first, preferring items the recipe does not otherwise need and using a needed resref only from the surplus (`ReserveStandIns`). Manufacturer and common traits do not count. The part's rare trait becomes a hull trait like any traited component's.
  - **Cost (material credits, pre-revamp / with the rare component / with the cheapest-dearest slot or stand-in):** Aurek, Sith Fighter, Cutlass 420 / - / 381-419 (0.91-1.00x); Eclipse, Raider Prowler 420 / - / 444-457 (1.06-1.09x); Chela Bomber 420 / - / 459-487 (1.09-1.16x); Basilisk, Jedi Transport 948 / - / 919-999 (0.97-1.05x); Lambda Shuttle 948 / - / 980-1,035 (1.03-1.09x); Barracuda 948 / - / 958-1,013 (1.01-1.07x); Raider Marauder 1,890 / - / 1,874-1,957 (0.99-1.04x); Hutt Grand Barge 1,890 / - / 1,871-1,954 (0.99-1.03x); Republic Centurion 3,945 / 4,758 (1.21x) / 3,736-4,995 (0.95-1.27x). `catalog.ships` asserts the band and `catalog.advanced_cost_rows` writes this table to Ship Components (Revamp).
- **Capital parts as the top band** (section 14): Capital hulls take 2 Capital Class Structural Components (`capc_struc`, Smithery: 2 tier 5 Hull Frames + 1 Refined Ardanium), a Capital Class Reactor (`capc_react`, Engineering: a tier 5 Ship Reactor + 1 Refined Ardanium) and Capital Class Thrusters (`capc_thrust`, Smithery: a tier 5 Thruster Assembly + 1 Refined Ardanium) instead of their 4 Hull Frames, Ship Reactor and Thruster Assembly, and five fewer Refined Gostian per part (all 20). Material value is unchanged: 4,825-5,025 per Capital hull, 1.22-1.27x the pre-component value (W11e band). Recipe level 45 (the Standard Capital hull's), category Capital Construction, `RecipeType` 7793-7795 (after 7792). The three items keep their blueprints; their Cost is now the bill's value (900 / 663 / 641, was 2,500), so a module repack is needed. The legacy level 52 recipes for these three (`CapitalConstruction`, `CapitalReactor`, `CapitalThruster`) are inactive; the other legacy capital parts are unchanged. A part keeps the traits of the tier 5 components it consumes (`ShipComponents.InheritsTraits`, `CarriesTraits`), so a manufacturer or rare trait still reaches a Capital hull.
- **Manufacturer components at the stores** (Appendix D): W15a's stores already sold each faction maker's component; the Smugglers' and the Hutt Cartel's stores now also sell the Chiss Sensor Array (the black markets at Smuggler's Moon and Tatooine; `FactionAgentTuning.BlackMarketMakers`, `agents.BLACK_MARKET_MAKERS`) at the same 25 tokens and Honored gate. The Raider Refit Hull stays Wild Space only. Token income is unchanged (no mission or price changed).
- **Not built (owner decision needed):**
  - **Ship Schematics for makers without an Advanced ship:** Czerka and Chiss have no Advanced ship in the 50-ship roster (Appendix C), so there is no deed recipe for a schematic to unlock. Giving them one is a new hull. Once one exists, the generators give it a schematic and the Czerka store (Exalted) and the data-site pools pick it up with no other change.
  - **Capital Industrial role:** still out of scope (section 21): a proposal with no numbers that needs new mechanics (refining aboard, a miners' yield aura) and would convert two migrated live capitals.
- **Bible:** regenerate Ship Components (Revamp) (rare component and capital part tables, notes), Ship Rewards (Revamp) (rare component and manufacturer component notes), Faction Agents (Revamp) (store rows), and the Engineering and Smithery Recipes rows (`bible_recipes.py`: 13 Advanced and 11 Capital deed bills, 3 capital part rows). `CombatUpgradeBibleRecipeParityTests` fails until then.
- **Balance:** no combat input changed (`checks.py` sections A-P unchanged); the income tables of section 19 are unchanged (no reward, price or credit model moved).
- **Tests:** `ShipComponentTests` (the rare-trait slot and the Capital rare component, the cost band for every Advanced hull, capital parts and their value, the stand-in rules, the window wiring), `FactionAgentTests` (black-market stores).
- **Needs client evidence:** crafting an Advanced deed with a Thorilide Core and with a rare-traited stand-in (the hull trait on the deed), crafting the capital parts and a Capital deed from them, and buying the Chiss Sensor Array at Smuggler's Moon.

**Added by the seat-skill update (W11n, the open owner decisions on the Engineering and Espionage rank bonuses and the cloak edge cases; the owner delegated the numbers):**

- **Rule:** a ground skill's rank adds to the modules its seat runs for whoever works them: the crew member at the module's seat, otherwise the pilot, solo pilots included (`Space.GetSeatSkillBonusPercent`, through `SpaceCrew.GetStatSource`). A droid, an NPC ship and a DM add nothing. Every bonus grows evenly per rank and stops at rank 50 (`ShipSeatSkillRules`, `Service/SpaceService`).
- **Numbers** (decided against existing standards):
  - **Engineering, module output:** Shield and Hull Repairer and Repair Field repairs and the Hypermatter Injector's capacitor +0.2% per rank, up to +10% at 50. Below the Command style's Repair Efficiency line (+5/10/15% for three perk ranks), so 50 ranks of a ground skill a crafter already has never outweigh an SP purchase. Applied at the module (`RevampModuleDefinition`: `Repairer`, `RepairField`, `HypermatterInjector`) before the station's effectiveness and Repair Efficiency; Emergency Repair, an ability, takes none.
  - **Engineering, power routing:** the routing's gain +0.5% per rank, up to +25% at 50, the size of Leadership's bonus to the Command style's orders. At 50: Weapons 31% less weapon capacitor, Shields +19% shield regen, Engines +25% speed. The penalties (weapon damage -5%, shield regen -10%, speed -10%) never change, so no mode kills faster than Balanced. `Space.SetPowerRouting` saves the Engineer's bonus as `ShipStatus.PowerRoutingBonusPercent` with the mode (saved data reads 0, no migration); every reset to Balanced (launch, the Engineer leaving, destruction) clears it; `PowerRoutingRules` takes it for capacitor cost, shield regen, speed, the HUD speed and the Engineer's message. The routing feats' TLK descriptions keep the base numbers (a hak change, not made here).
  - **Espionage, E-War:** Jammer and E-War Array Accuracy penalty and Target Painter damage taken +0.2% per rank, up to +10% at 50 (Mk X jam -22 to -24, paint +10% to +11%), the same size as Engineering's module bonus. Applied before the station's effectiveness (`Space.ApplyJam`, `ApplyPaint`, `EWarArray`). The Ion Web and Interdictor are not strengthened (section 4 lists jam and paint only).
  - **Espionage, cloak reveal:** +1% per rank, up to +50% at 50, the Probe Launcher's scan bonus: Mk I 10 m to 15 m, Mk X and the Barracuda 5 m to 7.5 m. A percent of the device's range keeps a better device harder to see at every rank. The observing ship uses its best rank, its pilot's or its Sensors officer's (`ShipSeatSkillRules.ShipEspionageRank`); a seated crew member looks through their own ship's sensors; NPC ships, droids and anyone not flying have none. `Space.IsShipHiddenFrom` and the cloak tick's visibility refresh both go through `IsCloakHiddenFrom`, which reads the rank only beyond the cloak's own range and within its largest stretch. Numbers in `cloak.py` (`CloakTuning.EspionageRevealPercentPerRank`, `EspionageRevealMaximumRank`).
  - Scan strength (+1% per Espionage rank) was built in W12a and is unchanged.
- **Crew cap:** the seat skills are the crew member's own, as perks are, and sit outside the 105% seat cap; only Leadership's crew bonus counts toward it. None adds weapon damage.
- **Cloak edge cases:**
  - **Docked:** `ShipCloakRules.GetStartError` refuses a ship docked aboard another ship or at a derelict ("You cannot cloak while your ship is docked aboard another ship."), checked first in `Space.CloakError` through `IsDockedAboardHost`. Docking already ended a cloak (leaving space clears every toggle) and no host can fit a device; this guard covers any path that leaves a docked ship flying.
  - **Server restart (decision: the cloak drops):** cloaks live only in memory (`ShipCloakBook`) and entering space clears every toggle. A crash could save the ghost effect on the character, so `ClearCloakState` now strips the cloak effect even with no cloak on record, and logging in (`Space.EnterServer`) strips it too. Visibility overrides do not survive a restart.
  - **Afterburner, Interdictor and Ion Web (checked, no gap):** cloaking switches the Afterburner off like every toggle, and no module runs while cloaking or cloaked. The web and hold need their target in sight (a toggle drops a hidden target, and a cloaked ship is no selected target), so they reach a cloaked ship only within the reveal range, Espionage included; there either breaks the cloak as damage does (W11k). A held ship cannot cloak.
- **Balance (`checks.py` section Q, new; A-P unchanged):** with Engineering 50 at the Engineer seat no crewed Frigate or Cruiser fight kills faster than Balanced with no rank (kill-time ratio 1.00), so section L's standing against N separate ships holds; the best job score is 110% of Balanced (Shields against 3 Elites; staying power only). A solo Gunship's Helm Shield Repairer at Engineering 50 gives at most 101% of the job without it (band 110%). At Espionage 50 a Fighter's Jammer or Target Painter swap stays within 85-106% of the Targeting Computer fit solo (band 110%), up to 117% of a party's job (no band). A crewed Cruiser at the cap with a Sensors Target Painter against 5 Fighters with one painter: 79 s against 80 s with no seat skills and 78 s against 79 s with Engineering and Espionage 50 on both sides (0.99 of their time either way), so the seat skills add nothing beyond noise and the cap stays level with N separate ships as in section G.
- **Bible:** Starships (Revamp) (Power routing and a new Seat skills row), Ship Modules (Revamp) (repairer, injector, Jammer, Target Painter, Repair Field and E-War Array rows), Cloaking (Revamp) (Espionage, docked, restart and interaction rows) and Space Balance (Revamp) section Q, all written by `bible_tabs.py`.
- **Tests:** `SpaceSeatSkillTests` (per-rank numbers, module mapping and wiring, routing gain and penalties, the saved bonus and its resets, the reveal stretch and its wiring, the docked refusal, the restart cleanup, the Afterburner, Ion Web and Interdictor interactions, the tool and plan entries); `CloakingTests` updated for the refactored wiring.
- **Needs client evidence:** a repair and an injector restore at Engineering 50 against 0; the routing message and HUD speed at Engineering 50; a jam and paint at Espionage 50; a cloaked ship revealed at 12-15 m by an Espionage 50 pilot and not by a rank-0 pilot; the docked refusal cannot be reached from the client (a docked ship cannot fly).

**Added by W11o (world and event features):** every number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/events.py` (incursion armadas, capital battles, zone hazards, pocket templates) or `rewards.py` (`WILD_ACES`); the Bible is not regenerated here (`bible_tabs.py --check` lists the tabs to regenerate: Space Enemies (Revamp), Ship Rewards (Revamp) and Events (Revamp); `checks.py` sections A-P are unchanged, since every armada preset equals its source ship's and no model input moved).

- **Incursion armadas (supersedes W15b's "no new creatures"):** each invader flies its own creatures, `t<tier>sitharm<code>` and `t<tier>mandoarm<code>` (codes ftr, int, bmb, gun, plt, cmd; 66 in all, `IncursionTuning.ArmadaShips`), copied from the tier's role ship the W7b way (same scripts, skin, level, loot locals and legacy ship item; `npc_catalog.hull_type` reads the code, so each preset equals its source's). Names come with the blueprint ("Sith Armada Fighter", "Mandalorian Armada Command Ship"); hulls: the Sith Armada flies the Imperial ship appearances and the Inquisitor Assault Cruiser, the Mandalorian Armada the Clan Zel Arun appearances and the Jahavey'ir Cruiser. `IncursionRules.WaveShips`/`BlockadeShips` take the invader; the tier 6 command ship stays `sithcap3`/`mandocap1`. The spawner no longer renames ships or swaps the capital's appearance. Registered in `NPCSpaceObjectDefinition.IncursionArmadas` and the creature palette (`creaturepalcus`).
- **Capital battle events (decision, the conservative version of section 11's encounter type):** every 4 hours (the first an hour after a restart), while no incursion runs, an armada battle group (the incursion command group of the orbit's tier: command ship and two gunships, Sith or Mandalorian at random) drops into Dathomir, Dantooine or Korriban Orbit for 30 minutes, then withdraws (`SpaceCapitalBattles.cs`, `CapitalBattleRules`, run from the incursion tick). Server-wide messages announce its arrival, destruction and withdrawal. It pays its ships' own loot and, from W11p, a contribution payout inside the incursion income band (see the W11p follow-ups below; checks O: a command group needs about 6 matched Fighters). In memory only.
- **Wild Space aces:** one per Wild Space system (`rewards.WILD_ACES`, six; tags `ace_ws*`): Kessa Vane (Vesh Pulsar, Interceptor), Old Mother Garrow (The Maw, Bomber), Jex Turlo (Ion Veil, Electronic Warfare), Brann Halvek (Korvax Magnetar, Sniper), Captain Seris Kol (Ember Giant, Fighter) and Warlord Tasker Vohl (Ship Graveyard, Fighter). Elites at the Rift Raiders' level 52 (hull / shield 554-719, between the T5 and T6 aces), copied from `rrgunship`, each flying a Hull Design no other creature uses (Vohl the unused Raider Marauder Pattern A). Loot: `SPACE_ELITE_WS, 66, 5` beside its own table (Design Schematic 30, Prototype Tech 10 in the Module Schematic slot, since 13 of the 16 named schematics already belong to one ace each, trophy 20, a tier 5 rare-trait component 25, the Rift Raider manufacturer component 15, the tier 5.5 Rift Key 15). Trophies `structure_0450`-`0455` (StructureType 450-455), achievements 218-223 (after the trade ones, so no id moved). Each system now reads its own creature table (`SPACE_WILD_SPACE_<ENVIRONMENT>`, the Rift Raiders at the W12c weights plus its ace at 600 of 71,640, 0.84%; `WildSpaceSystem.SpawnTable`): the six Wild Space `git` files changed (module repack).
- **Standalone minefields (decision):** `SPACE_MINEFIELD` waypoints (int local `HAZARD_TIER`): 3 in the Viscara Asteroid Belt (tier 1), 3 in the Mid Rim Nebula (tier 3) and 4 in the Korriban Debris Field (tier 5), the zones' lower tier. Each holds 4 `spacemine` mines on an 8 m ring (`SpaceHazards.cs`); a mine detonates on the first enemy ship within 5 m exactly as a minelayer's mine (W5b), fired by the mine itself with its tier Minelayer preset's mine DMG and launcher Attack, Accuracy and rank, and re-arms 300 s later. Mines drop nothing.
- **Pre-placed wrecks (decision):** `SPACE_WRECK_SITE` waypoints: one in each of the ten open orbits at its tier, two in the belt (tier 1) and the nebula (tier 3), three in the Debris Field and the Ship Graveyard (tier 5). Each keeps one "Drifting Wreck" (`Space.CreateWreck`: Medium Normal, 4 cycles, no rare find or data core, open to every ship), replaced 30 minutes after it is stripped or after its 60-minute life. A small share of a salvager's hour beside the kills' wrecks, so no income band moves.
- **Placement:** hazard waypoints stand on a 10 m grid at least 40 m inside the area edge and 45 m from every lane, dock, waypoint, planet blocker, decor placeable and trigger origin, outside the anti-spawn triggers, as far apart as possible (`ShipSalvageTests` re-checks 40 m). Coordinates are not verified in a client.
- **Rift Pocket per tier:** keys 1-5 open their own template (`space_riftpkt_t1`-`_t5`, copies of `space_riftpocket` with their own sky box, music and chamber layout; each chamber at least 40 m from the decor and 100 m from the others; listed in `module.ifo` and `Planet`), tier 5.5 keeps `space_riftpocket` (`RiftKeyDetail.Template`, `RiftRunTuning.TemplateRoomPoints`, `RiftRunRules.IsPocketTemplate`/`RoomPoint`). The DM Open/Close/List Pocket tools keep working: they open through `OpenRiftRun` and find runs by area.
- **Ordnance Stance (bug):** the ground status now applies its described -10% to single-target thrown damage (Throwing auto-attacks and single-target Throwing abilities) through `ThrowingSingleTargetDamagePercentAdjustment` (StatType 1285, `Combat.ApplyThrowingAbilityShapeDamageModifier`), kept apart from `SingleTargetDamagePercentAdjustment`, which space shots also read. Its two space launcher stats stay; `GenerateWeaponArchetypeImplementation.py` now emits the new stat too, so a rerun keeps the ground pair (the space pair is still hand-kept).
- **Tests:** `IncursionTests` (armada spawns, blueprints, presets, capital battles), `SpaceAceTests`, `ShipAccessRulesTests`, `RiftRunTests` (keys' Wild Space ace source, per-tier templates), `ShipSalvageTests` (hazard waypoints, field mines, wreck sites), `PilotingOrdnanceTests`, `CombatReleaseBalanceAuditTests`.
- **Needs:** a server deploy and a module repack (66 armada and 6 ace creatures, 6 trophies, the palette, 20 area `git` files, 5 pocket areas and `module.ifo`); no hak change. **Needs client evidence:** an armada wave and command ship, a capital battle's arrival and withdrawal, a Wild Space ace, a minefield detonation and re-arm, a wreck site salvage and refill, a tier pocket's sky and chambers, and the Ordnance Stance's single-target penalty in the combat log.
- **W11p follow-ups (capital battle payout, DM button, Wild Space hazards):** (1) The DM Tools Space tab has a Battle picker (Random orbit, Dathomir, Dantooine, Korriban) and a Start Capital Battle button (`Space.StartCapitalBattleAt`; refused while a battle or an incursion runs, the schedule untouched). (2) Destroying the whole battle group now pays each contributor as a cleared incursion does: damage is credited by persistent player id (`IncursionRules.CreditKill` into a battle-only book), online pilots are paid at once, offline pilots' spoils wait in `IncursionState.PendingBattlePayouts` and arrive at login (`CapitalBattleRules.BuildPayout`/`AddPending`); a withdrawal pays nothing. Pay is credits per point (T5 24.6, T6 67.5) plus one pull from the orbit's elite table with at least 1 point (events.py `CAPITAL_BATTLE_TARGET` 1.25: a solo pilot who destroys the group, 11 kill equivalents, 12.1 points, 11 modeled minutes, earns kill loot + pull + 298 credits at tier 5 and 817 at tier 6 = 125% of the tier's combat rate, inside the incursion's 100-140% band; `events.py --sim` prints the line). (3) Each of the six Wild Space systems holds 3 minefields (all six) and 2 wreck sites (the five without; the Ship Graveyard keeps its 3) at the Wild Space content tier 5, placed 45 m or more from every anchor, decor placeable and other hazard on the 10 m grid; a Wild Space mine hits as the Rift Raider Minelayer (`SpaceHazardRules.MinelayerTag(tier, wildSpace)`). Needs a server deploy and a module repack (the six Wild Space `git` files); the Bible's Events (Revamp) tab regenerates (capital battle payout rows). Needs client evidence: the Start Capital Battle button and a battle payout message, a Wild Space minefield and wreck site. Coordinates are not verified in a client.
- **Not built here:** new decor or tile layouts per pocket (the templates share The Maw's decor).

## 17. Numbers to draft for approval

Draft each in the Bible, check it with the combat simulator where it affects combat, and get owner approval before code.

- **Ships:** the ship formula; per-role baselines (slots, hull, shield, capacitor, speed, evasion, signature, cargo, interior limits, repair multiplier, built-in bonus); manufacturer modifiers; each ship's signature bonus size; Advanced multipliers; recipe levels; model scales.
- **Components:** recipes and quantities per ship; manufacturer component drop chances; rare component recipes.
- **Size rules:** hit-chance and damage penalty per size step.
- **Modules:** stats per family per rank step; capacitor cost, recast and range; Point-Defense and capital values; size scaling factors.
- **Components:** recipes, variant trait sizes, salvaged trait odds.
- **Perks:** every row for Ace, Ordnance, Bulwark and Command, plus the Gathering space perks.
- **Ship stats that replace rank:** hull Attack, Accuracy, Defense and Evasion per role and size; Attack and Accuracy per weapon step; Defense and Evasion per defensive step.
- **Ground-skill bonuses per rank:** Engineering (repair amount, capacitor transfer, power routing), Espionage (scan strength, jam and paint strength, reveal range) and Leadership (order and aura strength, command range, crew effectiveness). All drafted (Engineering and Espionage in W11n, section 16).
- **Combat:** STM costs, capacitor and shield regen formulas, crit factors.
- **Activation:** cycle time and capacitor per cycle for every family once the global recast is gone, and the auto-fire loop interval.
- **Crew:** droid crew effectiveness (the best industrial droid at about 75% of a player of matching rank, a tier-1 combat droid far below), industrial part recipes (about half the materials of combat parts), power-routing amounts.
- **Enemies:** space NPC stat presets per role and tier, ambush odds, convoy and patrol sizes.
- **Trade:**
  - Commodity base prices, flooding and recovery rates, exchange fee.
  - Freight deposits and pay, contraband scan odds and fines, tariff cap.
  - XP per delivery, standing gains.
  - The measured combat baseline and the resulting targets.
- **Repairs:** cost per hull size.
- **Refit:** fee.
- **Ship transfer:** fee by hull size.
- **Dockhand store:** prices for its stock (section 14).
- **Wild Space:** environment effect sizes, rift lifetimes, mass budgets and collapse damage, Rift Raider presets, loot tables.
- **Scanning:** signal gained per cycle, spawn rates per signature type, Sensor Sweep range and cooldown, Jump to Signature spool.
- **Stations:** Station Core recipe, Shipyard fee cap, anchor site count per area, Asteroid Drill output, Shipyard build times, Jump Beacon fuel cost and hull caps.
- **Holdings:** price, upkeep, output, upgrade costs, Processing Plant recipes, event odds, droid boost.
- **Droids:** Industry per industrial part, stat cuts, instruction effects, derelict salvage team timer and yield.
- **Slicing:** Data Analyzer range, slicing tiers per zone for data cans, locked caches, data cores and space lockboxes, space lockbox drop rates, customs spoof tier per patrol, space slicing XP and reward odds.
- **Activities:** agent mission pay and token prices, incursion schedule and rewards, Rift Run timers and loot, cloak speed penalty and reveal range.

## 18. Workstreams and order

Everything merges to one integration branch and releases together.

- **Lore review first:** before W0, the DMs approve the Wild Space faction and system names, all 50 ship names and the faction agent setup. Until then, every faction, system and ship name in this plan is a working name.
- **Branching:**
  - Create `feature/space-revamp` from up-to-date `origin/master`.
  - Each workstream slice goes on its own branch and pull request into it, with the Codex review loop (`PullRequests.md`).
  - **Bible edits happen on one branch only**, because separate branches that edit the `.xlsx` cannot be merged. Make them on the integration branch, one at a time.
  - Done: the integration branch `feature/space-revamp` started from the plan branch (`claude/festive-edison-bbnnxi`), which carried the proposed space perk tabs. If master's Bible changes in the meantime, redo the tabs on the new base with the same rows instead of merging the binary.
  - Merge `origin/master` into the integration branch regularly.
  - The final pull request goes to `master`.

| # | Workstream | Contents | Needs |
|---|---|---|---|
| W0 | Bible drafts | Piloting (Revamp) perk tab with four styles (drafted as Proposed on the plan branch); Starships tab rewrite (hulls, modules, components, traits); space enemy sections; trade tab (commodities, prices) | Owner approval gates W2-W9 numbers |
| W1 | Foundations | Piloting gating and seat skills, space `StatType`s, resistance types, High/Mid/Low and slot tags in `ShipStatus`, removing configs, size x role data model, `RequiresSkill` on space items | - |
| W2 | Combat rules | Shared crit, resist, status and threat; attribute mapping; regen; toggle and auto-fire loop; Ship HUD; PvP; escape pods; repair bills | W1 |
| W3 | Ships and modules | 50 ships (Appendix C) with model scales, Hull Designs and Refit, about 20 module families x rank steps, size scaling, icons with lit variants, store, component inputs for the existing Engineering recipes, ship transfers and market sales | W1, W0 |
| W4 | Perks and abilities | Four Piloting styles, Gathering space perks, space abilities and IDs | W1, W2, W0 |
| W5 | Enemies | NPC conversion, role AI, new groups, aces, encounters, spawn tables, DM Space tab | W2, W3 |
| W6 | World | Salvage and wrecks, gas harvesting, new materials, 3 zones (Module JSON), station flags, starlanes, docking limits | W3 |
| W7 | Trade | Cargo data, exchange NUI, pricing service, HoloNet board, contraband and customs, pirates, freight contracts, factions, city tariffs, trade XP | W3, W5, W6 |
| W8 | Crew | Stations, consoles, `AttachCamera` and hidden crew, droid crew, permissions, power routing, Command crew bonuses, Engineering, Espionage and Leadership seat bonuses, ship-to-ship docking and the Dock permission | W2, W3, W4 |
| W9 | Crafting and rewards | Component layer and traits, recipes, research blueprints, named modules, Design Schematics, trophies and decor, ammo variants, achievements | W3, W6 |
| W10 | Migration | Rebuild Token, perk refunds, item and ship conversion, stamp cleanup, design unlocks, notices | W1, W3, W9 |
| W12 | Wild Space and exploration | 6 Wild Space areas and environments, rifts, Rift Raiders, Probe Launcher, signatures, Jump to Signature, Sensor Sweep, data/cache/gas/combat sites, Scanner HUD tab, space slicing (Data Analyzer, new slicing sources, space reward tables, customs spoofing) | W2, W3, W5 |
| W13 | Stations | Station city layout, Station Core, anchor sites, deck areas, Shipyard, Asteroid Drill, Jump Beacons, Station Fuel, passenger shuttle route | W3, W7 |
| W14 | Holdings and droids | Holdings terminal, types, upgrades, production chains, events, ground sites; industrial droid parts, Industry stat, AI instructions, droid bays | W7, W9 |
| W15 | Activities | Derelict boarding (including contested derelicts and droid salvage teams), faction agents and token stores, incursions, Rift Runs | W5, W7, W12 |
| W16 | Cloaking | Cloaking Device, reveal rules, stealth unblock in space | W2, W12 |
| W11 | Release | Balance pass, client evidence, docs (section 19), player release notes | All |

**Suggested order:** W0 and W1 first, then W2 and W3, then W4-W9 and W12-W16 in parallel slices, then W10, then W11.

**W0 status:** the combat numbers (hulls, the 50 ships, modules, size rules, space enemies) are in the Bible's Starships, Ship Modules, Space Enemies and Space Balance (Revamp) tabs, generated and simulator-checked by `tools/SpaceRevampBalance`. Trade, component and economy numbers are drafted with their own workstreams (W7, W9), since nothing else depends on them.

## 19. Verification

- **Build and tests:**
  - Build once with `-p:RunPostBuildEvent=Never -v q -clp:ErrorsOnly`.
  - Run focused NUnit filters for each slice.
  - Add tests for: rank gating, size rules, slot rows, migration (refunds, item mapping, ship rebuild), seat skill checks (Piloting, Engineering, Espionage) and the Engineering, Espionage and Leadership bonuses, Piloting rank changing no combat stat, trade pricing and flooding, cargo spill, customs, XP split and range, PvP gating, escape pods, every auto-off trigger, support-module target locks and Retarget, All Weapons, space slicing (tier gate, Sensors crew slicing, Espionage XP, customs spoof outcomes), mining yield into ship storage (and the overflow to inventory), Shipyard access and fee, ship transfers and market sales (every structure re-owned, blocking conditions, designs not shared, permissions reset, migrated interiors kept).
- **Unit test coverage (checked by the gaps pass, W11d):** rank gating `ShipSkillGatingTests`; size rules `ShipShotBuilderTests`, `SpaceCombatMathTests`; slot rows `RevampCatalogTests`, `ShipStatCalculatorTests`; migration `SpaceItemMigrationTests`; seat skill checks and Leadership `SpaceCrewStationTests`; Piloting rank adds no combat stat `SpaceRevampBugListTests` (new); trade pricing, flooding and cargo spill `TradeTests`; customs `TradeRiskTests`; XP split `SpaceCrewStationTests` and range `SpaceRevampBugListTests` (new); PvP `SpacePvpRulesTests`; escape pods `SpaceDeathRulesTests`, `SpaceDeathWiringTests`; auto-off `ShipToggleRulesTests`, `CloakingTests`, `SpaceDroidCrewTests`; target locks, Retarget and All Weapons `ShipHudRulesTests`; space slicing `SpaceSlicingTests`; mining yield and overflow `ShipSalvageTests`; Shipyard `StationTests`, `StationBuildingTests`; ship transfers and market sales `ShipTransferTests` (new); Cargo Expander `CargoExpanderTests` (new). **No tests:** the Engineering and Espionage rank bonuses, which are not built (no numbers yet).
- **Engine tests** (`EngineTesting.md`) for:
  - Entering space with Mid slots.
  - Crew boarding, camera attach and re-attach after a starlane jump.
  - Droid crew.
  - Wrecks and salvage.
  - The saved-data item sweep.
- **Reward parity:** before release, credits per hour and loot value from space content at each tier must land within about 15% of ground content at the same tier. Measure both with the same method and record the results.
- **Combat simulator:** extend `SWLOR.CombatSim` with the ship model (the rules are shared). Report seeded results at entry, middle and endgame ranks for:
  - Damage pilot vs tank vs support.
  - Small vs Large vs Capital under the size rules.
  - A crewed capital vs the same pilots in separate ships.
  - The solo target at every tier: a pilot at that tier's rank with one weapon style (60 SP) solos all normal content and most elites in any hull their rank allows, sustained by shield regen, rank-0 repairers and consumables. Bulwark, Command and the seat skills are never required.
  - The Advanced ships in each role: each lands within 10% of the others at that role's job.
  - A melee build (MGT, PER) on launchers vs a ranged build (PER, AGI) on energy weapons at equal ranks.
  - **Fight length:** a matched solo pilot (same rank and attributes as the ground run) kills every normal space role at each tier within the ground's normal-enemy time at that tier, measured with the same simulator settings. No normal role takes more than 1.5x as long as the fastest one; on the ground, Melee, Ranged and Controller normals land within about 10% of each other. Capitals are compared with ground elites and bosses at the same tier.
- **Fight-length baseline (today, measured 2026-10-22):** ground from `SWLOR.CombatSim` (a longsword autoattacking a same-level Normal Melee enemy from the Bible's Enemy Stat Presets, attributes 16 + 0.2 x rank, 300 runs); space from a model of today's rules (the shared hit and damage formulas, the 2 s global recast, per-module recasts, shield before hull, halved ion overflow, NPC shield regen and repairers, real player ships, modules and configs, real NPC pilots and loadouts, perfect play, the NPC not fighting back). Medians:

| Tier (rank) | Ground, autoattacks | Ground, with abilities (rank 10-50) | Space fighter / interceptor | Space gunship |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T1 (5) | 18 s | 19 s | 22 / 26 s | 175 s |
| T2 (15) | 34 s | 25 s | 28 / 30 s | 85 s |
| T3 (25) | 47 s | 25 s | 32 / 34 s | 75 s |
| T4 (35) | 47 s | 25 s | 28 / 30 s | 59 s |
| T5 (45) | 51 s | 29 s | 36 / 38 s | 83 s |

- A T5 platform takes 90 s and a T5 capital about 340 s solo. Fighters and interceptors are 85% of space spawns; gunships, cargo haulers and platforms about 13%.
- Today's slow fights come from two rules this plan replaces: the 2 s global recast caps a ship at one shot per 2 s however many weapons it carries, and gunships, platforms and cargo haulers regain 1 shield per second (fighters 1 per 6 s), some with repairers on top.
- **Client evidence** (`InEngineVisualVerification.md`) for:
  - The Mid hotbar row.
  - Lit and unlit module icons, and a module switching itself off.
  - The Ship HUD with a support module retargeted, and its Stats tab.

  - Every Hull Design at ship scale.

| Strip mining, Industrial, no perks (best area of the tier) | - | - | 7,973 (94%) | 8,060 (107%) | 9,499 (102%) | 8,965 (40%) | 8,507 (78%) | about 90-100% |
| Mining with Prospector III and Rapid Extraction II | 5,202 (128%) | 8,047 (128%) | 9,829 (116%) | 9,328 (123%) | 12,227 (131%) | 12,745 (57%) | 10,814 (99%) | at most 140% |
| Strip mining with Prospector III and Rapid Extraction II | - | - | 10,164 (120%) | 10,276 (136%) | 12,292 (132%) | 11,451 (52%) | 10,865 (99%) | at most 140% |
| Mining, best pilot build (also Deep Core II, a +5 rare-find module, Prospecting Protocol II) | 5,424 (134%) | 8,527 (136%) | 10,415 (123%) | 9,877 (131%) | 12,947 (139%) | 13,497 (61%) | 11,450 (105%) | at most 140% |
| Strip mining, best pilot build (also Deep Core II, a +5 rare-find module, Prospecting Protocol II) | - | - | 10,417 (123%) | 10,530 (139%) | 12,596 (135%) | 11,734 (53%) | 11,134 (102%) | at most 140% |
| Mining, best pilot build and a best bay droid's ore (Industry 100, Efficiency Protocol II) | 5,424 (134%) | 9,637 (154%) | 11,771 (138%) | 11,162 (148%) | 14,632 (157%) | 15,254 (69%) | 12,941 (118%) | on top of the band (droid) |

  - **Passive income a day:** an account's three holdings average 30 minutes of their tier's combat at level 1 and 84 at their best (level 3, best droid, Processing Plant); three of the best type 90.0 (three Desalination Plants), the three best types 88 (80 after events). Review 2026-10-30: the limit is per account and upkeep 17% (was 15%: 31, 86, 91.8 and 90). An Asteroid Drill is the city's: 7-14 minutes of its system's combat a day without a droid, 12-25 with the best droid, before the city's 5,000 credits a day of upkeep.
  - **Outliers fixed:** contested derelicts at T3-T4 paid 138-142% because the wave step's floor (10%) held them above the solve; the floor is now 5% (`derelicts.WAVE_STEP_MINIMUM`), 131-134%. Two active incursion hours paid 140-141%; the region reward is now 0.35 hours of combat (`events.REGION_HOURS`, was 0.4), 137-140%, and four hours stay 130%. Three holdings at their best averaged 91 minutes a day (the best three types 96); a Processing Plant now refines 35% of the day's output (`holdings.PLANT_SHARE`, was 50%), 86 and 90.
  - **Kills an hour (review 2026-10-30, `kills.py --sim`; open, the lead's decision):** every income band above reads trade.py's assumed 60 kills an hour. Derived from checks.py section A's kill times (12.7 / 24.2 / 33.1 / 34.3 / 34.3 s at tiers 1-5, the orbit's Normal ships by spawn weight), the nearest Normal ship at half an orbit's 45-ship density (35 m, inside the Laser Cannon's 40 m, so no travel) and 4 s to retarget, a pilot who never waits kills 215 / 128 / 97 / 94 / 94 an hour (+57% to +259%); one who waits for the shield to regenerate what each fight took (about 20% of hull and shield) kills 51 / 42 / 41 / 39 / 40 (-15% to -35%). Both bounds are more than 15% off 60 at nearly every tier, so nothing was retuned: a higher rate raises the combat baseline and lowers every other activity's share (mining, trade, exploring, slicing, agents, derelicts, incursions, Rift Runs, Wild Space), a lower rate does the reverse. Looting, ships that aggro in pairs and docking to repair are not counted.
  - **Full production cost (review fix 2026-10-30, `production.py --sim`):** catalog.py compares raw material value only. The full cost adds every intermediate craft (components, capital parts, rare components) and its failures: a failed craft loses each component stack at 65% (`CraftViewModel.ProcessFailure`, kept by the crafting revamp's plan), so a craft at success rate s consumes inputs x (1 + 0.65 (1 - s) / s), and a hull or module failure loses 65% of its components with their own losses inside. Success rates are the crafting revamp's CraftSim parity tables (mid gear, rank at the recipe level): today's minigame 96-99% below level 41, 87% (Engineering) and 73% (Smithery) at 41-53; the revamp's 95-100%. The band (at most 1.27x the pre-revamp recipe on the same footing) is held at the revamp's rates, since it ships first. Blueprint fees (80 x perk level^2.2 x blueprint level^2 a craft: 80 / 367 / 896 / 1,688 / 2,759 at level 1) and the Shipyard fee (0-50,000, Capital hulls, set by the station's mayor) are optional and shown, not banded. Hulls below level 41 cost 1-4% over raw today and 0-3% with the revamp; Capital hulls 0.5% with the revamp but 24-26% today, because their capital parts are Smithery crafts at level 45 (Capital hulls 1.38-1.43x their pre-revamp recipe with today's minigame; open until the crafting revamp ships). Two things broke the band and are fixed: the Mining Lasers the dockhand sells (ranks 0-40, the salvage and gas modules with them) cost 1.93x (ranks 0-15) and 3.82x (ranks 40-45) their pre-component recipe because the Extraction Head took aluminum, steel or diamond; every tier now takes the tier's ground refined ore (1.00x). Cruisers cost 1.274x raw and 1.28x on full cost; a Standard Capital hull now takes one High Quality Electronics fewer (`components.CAPITAL_ELECTRONICS_SAVED`): 4,762-4,962 (1.21-1.26x raw, at most 1.26x full). Every other dockhand item is 0.66-1.09x; module ranks above the starters stay dearer where a gem or metal signature makes them so (section 14, "Cost"), up to 5.7x at rank 40-45. Ammo is one craft (+0-10% on raw).
  - **Below the band on purpose:** freight contracts (74% of T3; no cargo is bought, plan section 13's 75% target) and derelict salvage teams (74-77% of combat, 64-66% of boarding; a droid team does the work, section 28). Short participation pays more per hour where the reward is fixed: one incursion hour 157-159%, a Rift Run finished with half the timer left about 205%.
  - **Active mining (`mining.py`, from the code):** three matched Mining Lasers on one rock (the extraction hold is per ship), 1 ore a 6 s cycle and 1 asteroid unit (revamp lasers carry no Module Bonus and no hull sets Industrial), 1d4 + 2 units a rock (sized by tier, below), the nearest of half an area's asteroids at the PC run rate (4 m/s) x the hull's speed less the 15 m range, plus 4 s to retarget: about 820-910 ore an hour at full-size rocks, 2.3x the old assumed 360. At the 40% store price the asteroid tables paid 91-142% of combat at tiers 1-3 but 274-397% at tiers 4-5, 494% in Wild Space and 635% in the Korriban Debris Field, and strip miners 3-17x, mostly from metal and gem by-products (a diamond is 1,300 credits beside 38 for Gostian ore) and Ardanium rocks. Changed the ore value, not the cycle or the yield: `mining.py` now owns the sixteen asteroid loot tables. From tier 3 the ore rows carry the weight (Idailia 240, Barinium 480, Gostian 960 beside a by-product of 2 and a rare row of 1, so by-products are 0.3-1.2% of picks instead of 6-9%), an Ardanium rock gives Ardanium one pick in nine (Gostian otherwise), strip tables equal the laser tables (they were 4-5x richer), and the Debris Field spawns Idailia, Barinium and Gostian rocks 25 each and Ardanium 4 (Gostian alone at a zone's density paid 148%). Ore item values stay those of the matching ground ores. That brought tiers 1-5 to 99-138%. **Prospector and rock size (owner decision, W11e):** Prospector I-III add 5 / 10 / 15% to each cycle's ore instead of +1 / +2 / +3 (which quadrupled a 1-ore cycle: 215-315% of combat), the fraction carried on the ship between cycles (`SpaceCombatMath.MiningYield`, local `SPACE_MINING_YIELD_CARRY`); a cycle still uses the base amount's units. Ore values climb faster than the tier combat rates (Gostian 38 credits a unit beside Tilarium 8, against 9,347 and 4,055 an hour), so higher rocks hold fewer units: 1d4 + 2 at 85 / 100 / 70 / 60 / 55% by asteroid tier, rounded half up, at least 1 (`SpaceCombatMath.AsteroidUnits`, generated `SpaceMiningTuning`). Strip tables carry 3x the by-products of tier 3-5 rocks, to keep a 10 s strip level with three lasers. A no-perk miner now earns 90-102% of tier 1-5 combat (strip 94-107%), Prospector III with Rapid Extraction II 1.26-1.29x that (116-131%, strip 120-136%). Below the band: Wild Space 78% (its combat pays 17% more), the Mid Rim Nebula 74% (a gas zone), Smuggler's Moon 58% (tier 1-2 rocks) and tier 6 46% (no tier 6 ore; left as it is). A best bay droid adds about 13% on top of the perks. **Rare metal (review fix 2026-10-30):** `mining.py` now models the rare roll with the best legal rare-metal points (Deep Core II, a +5 RareFind Extraction Head and, on Mining Barge and Industrial hulls, Prospecting Protocol II; a mining module takes one Extraction Head, so one trait) and `mining.py --sim` exits 1 when that pilot build passes 140%: lasers 105-139% at tiers 1-5 and Wild Space, strip miners 102-139% (before: 78-101% at tiers 1-4, where Deep Core lowered income, and 456-633% at tier 5 and Wild Space). The tier 1 Fighter has no droid bay, so the model no longer gives it a bay droid. Open: a best bay droid's ore (Prospector's extra ore rounds up a cycle) still puts the dense zones and tier 4-5 orbits at 145-157% on top of the best pilot build, as the plan accepted for the perks; the band is held without it. Agents' mining quotas use the model's ore an hour per tier (`FactionAgentTuning.OrePerMinute`, 600-830), and the Asteroid Drill check its miner rate (0.20-0.25 of a mining hour a day, 0.33-0.43 with the best droid).
  - **Hull credit cost:** W9a's gem and metal signatures put hulls at up to 2.7x their pre-component material value. Where a hull recipe takes these components, the signature is now the tier's ground refined ore (Veldite ... Jasioclase, same item value as the space ore): Hull Frames from tier 3, Ordnance Assemblies and Sensor Suites in tiers 1-2, Extraction Heads in tiers 3-4 (`components.GROUND_REFINED`). Unit counts are unchanged. Material value by size, pre-W9a / W9a / now: Small 212-420 / 186-802 (up to 2.52x) / 186-407 (at most 1.00x); Medium 630-948 / 551-2,557 (up to 2.70x) / 551-937 (at most 1.19x); Large 1,422-1,890 / 2,998-4,530 (2.01-2.51x) / 1,378-1,850 (0.95-0.99x); Capital 3,945 / 9,625-9,825 (2.44-2.49x) / 4,825-5,025 (1.22-1.27x), since the 2026-10-30 review fix 4,762-4,962 (1.21-1.26x; below). Frigate 1,422 / 2,998 / 1,378; Cruiser 3,945 / 9,825 / 5,025 (now 4,962). The same swaps make the modules built from those components cheaper, and the cheaper components moved the generated slicing and site credits (the credit model values a component pick at its price).
  - **Exploring with store-valued schematics:** the store-price model now keeps every credit amount the generated one (before W11e it re-derived them, which hid the store value below tier 5: 123-137% at every tier with the generated credits, not only T5's 132%). The space slicing tables are out of 400 with the schematic and Prototype Tech picks at about their old weights out of 100 (a quarter of the old odds or less; the rest went to rare-trait components): exploring now pays 116.7-119.7% at store prices and 113.7-115.3% with schematics valued as components.
  - **Mid Rim Nebula:** its lane now leaves Hutlar Orbit (T3) from the Mon Cala arrival corner's no-spawn zone, lane at (272.5, 28.5), landing `STARLANE_LANDING_HUTLAR_4` at (275.5, 25.5); the nebula's lane back (306.4, 15.6) is "Starlane - Hutlar". Mid Rim 1's lane pair is gone. `trade.py --check` now compares `LANES` with the Module starlanes. Placement is a client check.
  - **Combat checks (`checks.py`, 150 runs):** all fifteen sections (A-O) reran identically before and after this pass and after merging master (W11e changes no combat input); every combat band holds.
- **Bible sync:** run `tools/UpdateCombatUpgradeAudit.ps1 -RefreshLocalBible` after every Bible edit. The Bible, C# descriptions, TLK and behavior must agree.
- **Docs after release:**
  - Move lasting rules into `ContentStandards.md` (hull, module and enemy standards; ship-access and docking rules) and `SkillPerkStandards.md` (space styles). Done (W11n): "Space content" in `ContentStandards.md`, "Space skills and styles" in `SkillPerkStandards.md`.
  - Fix `Builders.md`.
  - Delete this file.

## 20. Trellis2 request list

New models go through the Trellis2 pipeline (`Readmes/BlasterModelImport.md`) as a separate project. This plan ships without them.

- Space fauna: mynock swarms (latch on and drain capacitor) and a giant space slug (exogorth) in the Viscara Asteroid Belt.
- **Hull Designs for every ship:** several skins per ship, each usable only on that ship (section 7). Start with ships that have no launch skins.
- Trade containers and wreck variants, if the existing placeables look wrong at ship scale.
- Station exteriors for Small, Medium and Large stations, if scaling the existing `[mdrn]SpaceStation` placeable (placeables.2da row 1452) can't tell them apart.
- Rift Raider ships, if existing pirate models and the unused hak models (for example the Alien set, 10215-10217) don't give the coalition and its 2 refit hulls their own look.

## 21. Out of scope

- Any ground ability in space, or any space ability on the ground.
- Fuel for ordinary flight. Station Fuel is spent only by Jump Beacons (section 25).
- Capital jump drives, and jumping to hyperspace from anywhere (hyperdrive cooldowns, emergency or random jumps). Jump Beacons (section 25) and rifts (section 23) are the only travel beyond starlanes, so interdiction, pirate ambushes and trade risk keep working.
- Hostile boarding of player ships (a later pass). Ship-to-ship docking is friendly only (section 10).
- Interior Hull Designs (a later pass; section 7).
- Tier 6 content and the reserved ores (Ochne, Croknor, Hemorgite, Bisteiss). Wild Space is tier 5.5.
- Station raids and the Defense Grid building (a later pass).
- A Capital Industrial role (a later pass). The proposal: convert the Czerka Armored Transport and the Hutt Corvette into capital industrials that refine ore aboard, carry a large ore hold and droid bays, and boost nearby miners' yield. A passive that mines several asteroids at once was also suggested.
- Faction warfare, bounties on players, and racing.
- New 3D models (section 20).
- Player-posted hauling contracts. Items already teleport through the bank, so they add nothing.

## 22. Risks

- **Credits:** exchanges create credits. Measure the baseline before tuning, and watch the first weeks.
- **Crew engine edge cases:** camera loss on transitions, perception range, disconnects. These need engine tests and live client evidence.
- **Content volume:** about 280 module steps, 50 hulls, 4 styles, 3 orbit zones plus Wild Space, and new enemy groups. Slice pull requests by family so reviews stay readable.
- **Migration:** every `PlayerShip` record and every space item changes. Test the item sweep on a production-sized copy before release.
- **Balance of crewed ships against fleets,** and of Small hulls at endgame. The simulator checks both before release.
- **Expansion content volume:** 6 Wild Space systems, station deck areas, 9 holding sites, derelict templates and Rift Run pockets are all new areas. Build them from templates and reuse existing interiors wherever the plan allows.
- **More credit sources:** holdings, agents, incursions and Rift Runs all pay out. Measure them against the same combat baseline as trade (section 13) before release.

# Part II: Space Expansion

Everything in Part II ships in the same release as Part I. It builds on Part I's sizes, roles, toggles, salvage, trade, crew and droids.

## 23. Wild Space

- **Systems:** six persistent new areas that always exist, flagged as Wild Space. They are **tier 5.5**, matching the ground's tier 5.5: rank-50 gear and level-52 recipe unlocks. This is not tier 6, so the reserved ores stay unused.
- **Environments:** each system has one environmental effect, applied as `StatType` changes to every ship inside. Magnitudes are drafted in the Bible. Starting set:

| System type | Effect |
|---|---|
| Pulsar | Stronger shields, slower capacitor regen |
| Black Hole | More speed, less accuracy |
| Ion Storm | More EM damage, weaker shields |
| Magnetar | More damage, less evasion |
| Red Giant | Stronger overcharges and volleys, higher capacitor cost |
| Ship Graveyard | Richer salvage, weaker scanning |

- **Rifts:**
  - Hidden signatures (section 24) connect each Wild Space system to a random known orbit or to another Wild Space system. Each system keeps about 2 connections open, and new ones appear as old ones collapse.
  - Each rift has a lifetime of 4-24 hours and a size class (Small, Medium or Large) that caps the largest hull through it. Only Large rifts take Capital hulls.
  - Each rift has a mass budget, and every passage spends some of it by hull size.
  - On collapse, ships inside get a 2-minute warning. Then any ship still in a system with no open exit is thrown to a random known orbit with hull damage.
- **Rift Raiders:** a pirate coalition of cargo thieves and spice runners that preys on rift traffic, using all 8 role behaviors together. Its stats are drafted from the ground tier-5.5 presets. Their wrecks spill stolen cargo (section 13), some of it contraband, on top of normal salvage. Models come from existing pirate ships, unused hak ships, or Trellis2 (section 20).
- **Loot:** named rank-50 module recipe unlocks, Design Schematics, Thorilide, Aurodium, tier-5.5 Rift Keys, rare component traits, and **prototype tech** (stolen experimental parts). Prototype tech is a research input that improves blueprint bonuses (section 14).
- **Stations:** 1-2 anchor sites per system (section 25). Jump Beacons never connect into or out of Wild Space.
- **Higher stakes:** Wild Space is the only place a PvP death can drop modules (section 9).
- **Added by the Wild Space update (W12c):**
  - **Numbers:** every Wild Space number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/wildspace.py`, which generates `WildSpaceTuning` (`Service/SpaceService`) and writes the Bible's Wild Space (Revamp) tab (`wildspace_tab`, listed in `bible_tabs.py`). `wildspace.py --sim` prints the credits per hour; `checks.py` section N runs the environments through the simulator. Draft numbers, tune in testing.
  - **Tier 5.5 (decision):** a Wild Space area carries the local int `SPACE_TIER_TENTHS` = 55. Every tier-indexed table (site cache credits, slicing targets, wreck, scrap and salvager tiers, space lockboxes) uses tier 5 (`WildSpaceTuning.ContentTier`, `WildSpaceRules.ContentTier`), and the area's `WILD_SPACE` local adds the half step: the Rift Raiders (level 52), the Wild Space loot tables, the Wild Space only schematics, the 5.5 shown on the Scanner tab (`ScanSignature.WildSpace`, `ScanningRules.TierLabel`) and a signal of 240 x 5.5 x difficulty.
  - **Systems** (Module `are`/`git`/`gic`, copied from an orbit with its docks, starlanes, stations, beacons, triggers and waypoints removed, the stuck waypoint kept; working names): `space_wspulsar` "Wild Space - Vesh Pulsar" (Pulsar, from `space_dathomir`), `space_wsblkhole` "Wild Space - The Maw" (Black Hole, from `space_midrim2`), `space_wsionstorm` "Wild Space - Ion Veil" (Ion Storm, from `space_midrim1`), `space_wsmagnetar` "Wild Space - Korvax Magnetar" (Magnetar, from `hutlar_orbit`), `space_wsredgiant` "Wild Space - Ember Giant" (Red Giant, from `space_korriban`), `space_wsgraveyrd` "Wild Space - Ship Graveyard" (from `space_korrdebris`). Locals: `SPACE` 1, `WILD_SPACE` 1, `SPACE_TIER_TENTHS` 55, `WILD_SPACE_ENVIRONMENT` (`WildSpaceEnvironment` 1-6), `CREATURE_SPAWN_TABLE_ID` `SPACE_WILD_SPACE`, `RESOURCE_SPAWN_TABLE_ID` `SPACE_RESOURCES_KORRIBAN_DEBRIS` (tier 5 ores) and `SALVAGE_AURODIUM_PERCENT` 8 (the Graveyard 15). No `MAX_SHIP_SIZE`: rifts cap the hull. Registered in the module area list, `Planet`'s space areas, the toolset's Space category and `ScanningTuning.Areas` (`WildSpace = true`, 3 site signatures). PvP setting: the server default, as in the orbits. Achievement 214 (W9c) fires on entering, through the same `WILD_SPACE` local.
  - **Environments:** on entering, every ship (players and NPCs) takes its system's stat changes as a temporary stat modifier group (`Space.ApplyWildSpaceEnvironment`, OnAreaEnter; a pilot's a second after arriving, with a message naming the system, its effects and the PvP stakes); leaving the area removes them (OnAreaExit), and logging out clears them. New stats 1280-1284 (W9c may use 1260-1279): `SpaceShieldDamageTakenPercentAdjustment` (scales the shield points a hit costs, `ApplyShipDamage`), `SpaceCapacitorRegenPercentAdjustment`, `SpaceEmDamagePercentAdjustment`, `SpaceAbilityDamagePercentAdjustment` (ability shots: the overcharged shots and volleys) and `SpaceCapacitorCostPercentAdjustment` (`GetModuleCapacitorCost`); existing stats for the rest. Pulsar -12% shield damage taken, -25% capacitor regen; Black Hole +25% speed (`MovementSpeedPercentAdjustment`), -4% Accuracy (`SpaceAccuracyPercentAdjustment`); Ion Storm +25% EM damage, +15% shield damage taken; Magnetar +4% damage (`DamageDealtPercentAdjustment`), -3% Evasion (`EvasionPercentAdjustment`); Red Giant +20% ability damage, +20% module capacitor use; Ship Graveyard +1 salvage item per cycle (`SpaceSalvageYieldBonus`), +5 rare-trait chance (`SpaceRareTraitChance`), -25% scan strength (`SpaceScanStrengthPercent`). `checks.py` section N (a named-fit Fighter against each level 52 Normal role, the environment on both ships): Pulsar +8.2 to +9.1%, Black Hole +5.8 to +9.1%, Ion Storm -7.9 to -9.1%, Magnetar -6.7 to -9.4%, Red Giant and the Graveyard 0% (abilities, capacitor, salvage and scanning do not change an auto-fire fight), inside the +-10% band. The Raider Prowler's signature is now real: it ignores environment penalties and scans Wild Space 50% stronger (`WildSpaceRules.HullScanBonusPercent`).
  - **Rifts** (`RiftConnection`, `RiftRules`, `SpaceRifts.cs`): rifts keep weight 0 in the site spawner; the rift network places them. Every 15 s it warns, collapses and replaces: each Wild Space system keeps 2 open (a Wild Space to Wild Space rift counts for both), 60% to a random known orbit (the ten open orbits, not the zones) and otherwise another Wild Space system still short of its 2. Each end is a hidden Rift signature at a random point (`SignatureSiteRules.CreateRiftEnd`, tier 5.5 signal x 1.5: 1980), whose threat-level tooltip names the size, the largest hull and the far end. Lifetime 4-24 hours (uniform, to the minute). Size classes (decision on "caps the largest hull"): Small (weight 50, up to Medium hulls, mass 30), Medium (35, up to Large, 60), Large (15, every hull including Capital, 120). A passage spends Small 1, Medium 2, Large 5, Capital 15; a spent rift collapses after the warning. Passage: Jump to Signature on a located rift (the hotbar feat takes a rift at any distance; the hull and mass are checked before the spool and again at its end), then `CrossRift` moves the ship to 20 m from the far end, which the crossing ship then holds at 100% so its party can come back. Collapse: 2 minutes before, every pilot in the Wild Space system(s) it serves is warned; at collapse both ends go, and every ship there with no open rift that fits its hull and has the mass for it (logged-out ship clones too) is thrown to a random known orbit with its shield stripped and 25% of max hull lost (never below 1 hull). A collapsed connection's replacement opens 5 minutes later.
  - **Restart (decision):** rift state lives in memory. The first network pass after a restart opens every system's 2 rifts at once with fresh lifetimes. No ship is stranded: the boot already clears every starship's space position (`Property` clean-up), so a ship that was in Wild Space relaunches from its dock.
  - **Rift Raiders:** 9 creatures (Module/utc, copied from the tier 5 ships the W5b and W7b way: same scripts, Hostile, a Rift Raider description, an Appendix C pirate appearance) on the new level 52 skin `rrskin`: `rrfighter` (Cutlass, from `t5pirftr`), `rrinterceptor` (Prowler, from `t5pirint`), `rrbomber` (Barracuda), `rrsniper`, `rrewar`, `rrminelayer`, `rrgunship` (Marauder, from `t5pirgun`), `rrlogistics` and `rrcarrier` (Boss): all 8 W5b behaviors plus the pirate fighter and interceptor. Their presets come from `npc_catalog.py` (`hull_type` takes the `rr` prefix) at level 52, the ground presets carried on from level 50: `RevampNpcShipCatalog`, `NPCSpaceObjectDefinition.RiftRaiders`, the creature palette, the `SPACE_WILD_SPACE` spawn table (the open-orbit weights, the bomber at the role ships' 1920) and the DM Space tab's new "T5.5 Rift Raiders" faction (its pirate roles map to the raiders; it runs no convoys). A Rift Raider carrier launches Rift Raider fighters (`ShipNpcRoleRules.FighterTag`), and their wrecks yield the Rift Raider manufacturer components (`ShipManufacturerRules.WreckManufacturer`, W9c). A destroyed Rift Raider spills stolen cargo 20% of the time (`WildSpaceRules.RollStolenCargo`, through `Trade.SpillUnits`): one good of Moonspice (contraband, 30% of spills), Weapons Crates, Encrypted Data Chips, Reactor Cores, Medical Supplies or Czerka Machine Parts, about 327 base credits.
  - **Loot:** `SPACE_BASIC_WS` (tier 5 goods with Aurodium), `SPACE_BASIC_WS_RARES` (Thorilide, Prototype Tech, the Rift Key, gems), `SPACE_ELITE_WS` (adds Aurodium; rare: Thorilide, Prototype Tech, the Rift Key, the Rift Raider ships' Design and Ship Schematics) and `SPACE_BOSS_WS` (the 16 Module Schematics at 3 each, about 9% of pulls, Thorilide, Prototype Tech, Aurodium, the Rift Key and the Rift Raider schematics). Site caches and locked caches in Wild Space pull from `SPACE_ELITE_WS`; combat sites are Rift Raider squads. Wrecks in Wild Space (`ShipWreck.WildSpace`): rare finds draw on the Wild Space tables and add a Module Schematic (elite 5%, boss 10%) or Prototype Tech (10%, 15%) after the W9b shares, schematic pools include the Wild Space only ones (also for data cans, locked caches and data cores sliced there: `SlicingReward.RollSpace(..., wildSpace)`), and salvaged components roll rare traits 10 points more often. `rift_key_t55` "Rift Key (Tier 5.5)" (the keycard model 197, 2,000 credits) drops now at a low rate; Rift Runs (W15) will use it. Prototype Tech's sources are these tables and rare finds (W9c had already removed its `NO_ECONOMY`).
  - **Credits (decision):** Wild Space combat pays 10,955 an hour (143 loot + 39 stolen cargo per kill at 60 kills an hour, cargo sold at 60% of base), 120% of tier 5 combat (9,161): the higher-stakes zone. Rare-flagged drops (schematics, Prototype Tech, Thorilide, the Rift Key) are left out, as in the tier baselines. Exploring Wild Space sites uses the tier 5 cache credits and is not modeled separately.
  - **Tests:** `WildSpaceTests` (environments, stat math, wiring, tier 5.5, rift lifetime, size, mass, warning, ejection, restart seeding, destinations, rift ends, Rift Raider presets, blueprints and cargo, Wild Space loot, rare finds, slicing pools, the PvP module drop and achievement 214) and `ShipAccessRulesTests` (the areas' flags, no lanes or docks, registration, only these six carry `WILD_SPACE`).
  - **Not built here:** stations and anchor sites (W13), Rift Run use of the key (W15), a separate exploring payout model for Wild Space sites, client verification of the areas, environments, rifts and Rift Raiders, and lore review of the names.

## 24. Scanning, exploration and slicing

- **Probe Launcher** (Mid slot, Piloting, or Espionage at the Sensors seat; toggle): each cycle adds signal strength to every hidden signature in the area, system-wide.
- **Scan strength** grows with the Espionage rank of whoever runs the launcher (the pilot or the Sensors officer), a droid running Survey Protocol (section 27) and the system's environment.
- **Signature types:** rift, combat site, data site, cache site, gas site, derelict. They spawn at random points and stay hidden: **no object exists in the area until the signature reaches 100%**, so flying over the spot finds nothing.
- **Information grows with strength,** shown on a new Scanner tab of the Ship HUD:
  - 25%: type
  - 50%: tier
  - 75%: threat level and loot class
  - 100%: the site opens for the scanning party
- **Jump to Signature:** at 100%, anyone in the scanning party can micro-jump to the site after a 5-second spool. Damage and Interdictors interrupt the spool. Players outside the party must scan it themselves.
- **Sensor Sweep:** a pulse mode on the Probe Launcher with its own cooldown. It lists every ship within range, cloaked ships included.
- **Sites:**
  - **Combat sites:** waves and a boss at the system's tier.
  - **Data sites:** the **Data Analyzer** runs the existing slicing mini-game (`Service/SlicingService/`) on data cans. They yield blueprints, recipe unlocks and agent intel, which unlocks special agent missions.
  - **Cache sites:** hidden pirate caches. The Salvager (Gathering) works the open containers for prototype tech and components. Locked caches hold the site's best loot and need slicing.
  - **Gas sites:** the Gas Harvester.
  - **Derelicts:** section 28.
- **Despawning:** sites despawn once completed or after a timer.
- **Added by the scanning update (W12a):**
  - **Numbers:** every scanning number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/scanning.py`, which generates `ScanningTuning` (`Service/SpaceService`) and the Bible's Scanning (Revamp) tab (`bible_tabs.py`). Draft numbers, tune in testing. `scanning.py --sim` prints the payout against combat.
