  - **Probe Launcher:** family 43 (`ShipModuleFamily.ProbeLauncher`, `ShipModuleType.ProbeLauncher` 24), `sm_probe_00`-`sm_probe_45`, the Ship Modules tab's draft: Mid, Piloting (a Sensors seat checks Espionage at the same rank, as every station slot does), toggle, 10 s, 4 capacitor, scan strength 10 + 2 x rank, Sensor Sweep range 100 + 2 x rank m. Recipes are `RecipeType` 7488-7497 (`catalog.py` `COMPONENT_LATE_FAMILIES`, numbered after the component recipes so nothing moved), each with a Sensor Suite of its tier. Art: ESS9 model 238, action icon `iit_smprobe`. Self-centered toggle: `RunProbeCycle` adds the cycle's signal for the ship's pilot to every hidden signature in the area.
  - **Signal:** strength x (1 + bonuses / 100) x station effectiveness. Bonuses: +1% per Espionage rank of whoever runs it (the pilot, or the player at the Sensors seat; a droid has none), up to +50% at rank 50; the hull (Explorer +25%, Chiss +15%, Chiss Hyperion +25% more, and the Hyperion shows the tier from 25%); the environment through `StatType.SpaceScanStrengthPercent` (1450; W12c's systems set it); the droid Survey Protocol hook `SpaceCrew.GetDroidSurveyBonusPercent` (0 until W14). A signature needs 240 x tier x type difficulty (combat and gas 0.8, cache 1.0, derelict and data 1.2, rift 1.5): a probe at the tier's matched rank with no Espionage resolves difficulty 1.0 in 12 cycles (2 minutes).
  - **Signatures** (`ScanSignature`, `SignatureType`, `SignatureSiteRules`): in memory, gone on a restart. 3 at a time in each of the 10 orbits and 4 in each Small/Medium zone (`ScanningTuning.Areas`), at random walkmesh points. Weights: combat 35, cache 25, gas 20 (from tier 3, where the gas clouds are), derelict 20; data sites and rifts weigh 0 until W12b and W12c. Strength is per scanning ship; nothing spawns until one reaches 100%. A hidden signature fades after 60 minutes, an open site despawns 20 minutes after it opened or when completed, and a new signature appears 5 minutes later. Site ships still fighting when their site ends stay until nobody fights them.
  - **Scanner tab** (Ship HUD, third tab): every signature the viewer's party has signal on, strongest first: type at 25%, tier and the area's sector (3 x 3 grid) at 50%, threat at 75% (the loot class is in the tooltip), exact coordinates and an enabled Jump button at 100%. Below it the Sensor Sweep button and its last list.
  - **Jump to Signature:** feat 3358 (`FeatType.ShipJumpToSignature`, TLK 193148-193149, icon `ife_sigjump`, no spell row), added in `EnterSpaceMode` and put on the hotbar when the ship has a Probe Launcher; the hotbar feat jumps to the nearest located site more than 40 m away. Anyone whose party (any member) holds 100% on an open site may jump; outsiders must scan it themselves. A 5 s spool, broken by any shield or hull damage (`ApplyShipDamage`), then a micro-jump to 20 m from the site on the near side. `Space.IsShipInterdicted` is the Interdictor hook (false until it has a mechanic). `GeneratedFeatEnd` is 3358 in the three icon and feat tools.
  - **Sensor Sweep:** the Scanner tab's button. The pilot's best fitted Probe Launcher pulses for 10 capacitor on a 60 s cooldown and lists every ship within its sweep range (nearest first, at most 20, for 60 s), cloaked ships included through the `Space.IsShipCloaked` hook (the Cloaking Device fills it, section 32).
  - **Sites** (`SpaceScanSites.cs`): a combat site is a squad of the area's tier (`t<tier>fighter` x2 and an interceptor, plus a bomber from tier 3 and a gunship from tier 5); a gas site 5 gas clouds (`spc_gascloud_t3`, or `_t4` from tier 4); a derelict a Large Elite wreck of the tier (`Space.CreateWreck`, the scanning party has the claim) with defenses (the debris field's derelict turrets from tier 5, the tier's defense platform below). When the squad, defenses, wreck or clouds are all gone, a Site Cache (`spc_sig_cache`) appears; a cache site is a Hidden Cache from the start. Only the scanning party may open a cache: it pays the site's credits and one pull from `SPACE_ELITE_Tn` into the ship hold, and completes the site.
  - **Decision, cache and data sites:** cache sites are lootable caches now; data sites do not spawn until the Data Analyzer (W12b), so no scan is wasted on something that cannot be worked. The locked cache, the data can and their slicing are W12b.
  - **Payout:** each site's cache pays the site's time plus its share of the scan (the slowest type in play at a matched rank), 2 minutes of travel per area and 0.25 minutes of jump, at 115% of the tier's space combat credits per hour (trade.py's baseline; T3 8,500/h), less what the site already pays (the ships' own loot, the wreck's scrap, the gas). Exploring pays 114-115% of its tier's combat at every tier (T3 about 9,780/h). The premium stays inside 100-130% because exploring costs a Mid slot, scanning time with nothing to shoot and Espionage ranks, without pulling combat pilots off combat. Cache credits by tier (combat / cache / gas / derelict): T1 100 / 100 / - / 75, T2 200 / 225 / - / 325, T3 300 / 300 / 375 / 625, T4 250 / 225 / 275 / 225, T5 50 / 275 / 375 / 150, T6 600 / 825 / 1425 / 1850, each with one elite pull.
  - **Tests:** `ScanningTests` (signal and scaling, thresholds, hidden until 100%, party jump eligibility, spool interruption, sweep cooldown, spawning and despawning, payout per hour) and the engine test `ScanningEngineTests` (category Scanning).
  - **Not built here:** rifts and Wild Space environments (W12c), data sites, the Data Analyzer, locked caches and slicing (W12b), the droid Survey Protocol (W14), Interdictors and cloaking (their hooks only; both built since, W16 and W11k), Espionage XP for scanning (built in W11k), saving signatures over a restart, a DM "Place Signature" tool, client verification of the Scanner tab and the sites.

### Slicing in space

Espionage's Slicing perk works in space exactly as on the ground.

- **Data Analyzer:** a Mid-slot Espionage module (Engineering crafts it). Any ship can fit it, with no rank requirement. It is a one-press module that starts the mini-game on a target in range; range is a number to draft (section 17) and replaces the 5 m terminal range for space targets.
- **Who slices:** the pilot, or the crew member at the Sensors station with their own Slicing perk (section 10).
- **Rules kept from the ground:** `SlicingSession.ValidateStart` still requires a Slicing tier at or above the target's tier, and no slicing starts in combat. Wild Space targets are tier 5.
- **New sources:** add `SlicingSourceType` values for data cans, locked caches, wreck data cores and customs scans.
- **Targets:**
  - Data cans at data sites.
  - Locked caches at cache sites.
  - Data cores in elite, ace and boss wrecks (section 12).
  - Terminals and lockboxes inside derelicts (section 28).
  - Customs scans (section 13).
  - Data-heist missions for faction agents (section 29).
  - Space lockboxes dropped by NPC ships (section 12).
- **XP:** Espionage XP through `Skill.CalculateEspionageXP(PerkType.Slicing, tier, rank)`, never Piloting XP.
- **Rewards:** new space reward tables in `SlicingRewardCatalog` for each new source: Design Schematics, recipe unlocks, Ship Schematics, agent intel and components with rare traits. Ground lockbox and terminal rewards stay as they are. New items follow `EconomyRestrictedItems.md`.
- **Added by the space slicing update (W12b):**
  - **Numbers:** every space slicing number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/slicing.py`, which generates `SpaceSlicingTuning` (`Service/SlicingService`), `SpaceSlicingItems` (`Service/SpaceService`), the five space lockbox blueprints and the data can placeable, and the Bible's Slicing (Revamp) tab (`bible_tabs.py`). `slicing.py --sim` prints each source's value; `scanning.py --sim` counts data sites, locked caches, data cores and lockboxes in the payout. Draft numbers, tune in testing.
  - **Data Analyzer:** family 44 (`ShipModuleFamily.DataAnalyzer`, `ShipModuleType.DataAnalyzer` 25), one item `sm_dataan_00` "Data Analyzer" (`catalog.py` `SINGLE_STEP_FAMILIES`: rank 0, no RequiresSkill property, so any hull fits it), Mid, Espionage, one press, 6 s recast, 5 capacitor, range 30 m (drafted here for section 17; it replaces the ground's 5 m terminal range for space targets and is measured from the ship). Recipe `RecipeType.ShipDataAnalyzer00` 7532 (`catalog.py` `REWARD_LATE_FAMILIES`, after the blueprint update's 7498-7531; Engineering, level 2: a tier 1 Sensor Suite and Tilarium, the W9a mapping). Art: ESS9 model 240, action icon `iit_smdatanlz` with its cooldown frames. A press makes whoever runs it (`SpaceCrew.GetStatSource`: the pilot, or the Sensors crew member; a droid at the seat slices outright, W11n) start `SlicingSession` on the targeted data can, locked cache or wreck data core.
  - **Rules kept from the ground:** `SlicingSession.ValidateStart` keeps tiers 1-5, the Slicing rank gate and no slicing in combat; for space sources the slicer's ship counts too (`Space.IsSlicingShipInCombat`), so a Sensors crew member cannot slice while the ship fights. A space target's tier is its area's tier, at most 5; Wild Space targets are tier 5 (`SpaceSlicingRules.TargetTier`). Espionage XP is the ground's (`Skill.CalculateEspionageXP(PerkType.Slicing, tier, rank)`), never Piloting XP. The mini-game window uses the lockbox art for space lockboxes and the terminal art for the other space sources.
  - **Sources** (`SlicingSourceType` 3-7): `DataCan`, `LockedCache`, `WreckDataCore`, `CustomsScan`, `SpaceLockbox`.
    - **Data sites:** weight 15 (beside combat 35, cache 25, gas 20, derelict 20). A data site holds 3 data cans (`spc_datacan`, tag `spc_datacan`, appearance 6030 "[mdrn]Rusted, DataTerm"; a click targets it as a wreck) on a 12 m ring, sliceable by the scanning party only. When every can is sliced or destroyed the site cache appears, as at the other sites.
    - **Locked caches (decision):** 40% of cache sites are locked caches (`ScanSignature.Locked`, rolled when the signature spawns). The Scanner tab's loot line says it is locked. Using one only tells the pilot to slice it; a click targets it (the `spc_sig_cache` tag is registered as a space object, and only a locked cache gets the click script). Slicing it open pays `ScanningTuning.LockedCacheCredits`, the site's elite pull and a slicing reward, and completes the site; a failure that destroys it ends the site with nothing.
    - **Wreck data cores:** elite and boss wrecks, and aces through the creature local `SPACE_ACE` (the hook for W9 aces, none placed yet), carry one (`ShipWreck.HasDataCore`; a derelict's Large Elite wreck too). The killing party's 60 s claim covers it (`SpaceSlicingRules.CanSliceDataCore`, the pilot or the slicer counts). Slicing drains the core; the wreck stays for salvage.
    - **Customs scans:** "Slice the Scan" is enabled (`Trade.CanSliceCustomsScan`) when the pilot or the Sensors crew member has the Slicing rank for the patrol's tier (the system's tier, at most 5) and the ship is out of combat; whoever has it slices (the higher rank when both do). It needs no Data Analyzer and no range. The 30 s answer timer waits while the scan is sliced. Success is a clean scan (no fine, no confiscation, no standing lost). Failure, giving up, or the session ending any other way is being caught: the fine (or what the pilot carries, when less), the confiscation and the caught-smuggling standing loss (`SpaceSlicingRules.SpoofOutcome`).
    - **Space lockboxes:** `spc_lockbox_t1`-`_t5` "Sealed Ship Lockbox (Tier N)" (ESS9 base 523, one model 241 shared by the tiers as Ship Scrap shares one, action icon `iit_spclockbox`, cost 30-640), beside `lockbox_t1`-`t5`. A destroyed preset NPC ship drops one into its killer's hold (the killing blow's ship, else the top-threat ship; `Space.RollSpaceLockbox`) at 2% (normal), 8% (elite) or 20% (boss), of the ship's tier at most 5. The owner slices it later, out of combat, anywhere, as a ground lockbox (`SpaceLockboxItemDefinition`), and the rewards go to them.
  - **Rewards** (`SlicingRewardCatalog.GetSpaceCredits`/`GetSpaceBonusWeights`, `SlicingReward.RollSpace`; the ground tables are untouched): every success pays the source's credits at 75-125% and one bonus pick. Base credits by tier 1-5: data can, locked cache and data core 50 / 75 / 100 / 25 / 25, lockbox 25 / 50 / 50 / 25 / 25 (40% of what exploring pays over a board, 25% for a lockbox, capped so credits and the pick never pay more than the board; T4-T5 are low because their components are worth more). Picks: data can Design Schematic 10, recipe unlock 5, Ship Schematic 2, agent intel 20, component 41, rare-trait component 22; locked cache rare-trait component 35, component 30, ammo 20, Design Schematic 7, Prototype Tech 5, recipe unlock 3; data core Design Schematic 15, recipe unlock 8, Ship Schematic 4, Prototype Tech 5, rare-trait component 35, component 33 (the best schematic odds); lockbox component 35, rare-trait component 20, ammo 30, credits 15 (never schematics or cargo). Components are a salvage family's generic component of the tier with a common or rare trait. Ammo by tier: missiles and hypermatter capsules at T1-T2, missiles and proton bombs at T3-T4, proton bombs or an assault concussion missile at T5. Schematics are the blueprint update's items (`SlicingReward.GetSchematicPool`): Design Schematics (`dsgn_*`) and Ship Schematics (`schm_*`) for ships of the target's tier or lower, Wild Space only ones left out (`ShipRareFindRules`, as salvage rare finds give them); recipe unlocks are the Module Schematics (`recipe_<family>50`) and come only from tier 5 targets (decision: they unlock the named rank-50 modules); Prototype Tech is `proto_tech`. A pick with an empty pool (no Ship Schematic below tier 2, no recipe unlock below tier 5) gives a rare-trait component. Agent intel waits for W15 (`Space.AgentIntelAvailable` false, `GrantAgentIntel` the hook) and gives a rare-trait component until then. Site rewards go to the slicer (credits) and the ship's hold (items); lockbox rewards to the slicer.
  - **Payout** (`scanning.py --sim`): exploring stays at 114.3-115.4% of its tier's combat at every tier (T3 9,802/h against 8,500). The model counts a schematic or Prototype Tech pick as one component, as it does not count the salvage rare finds that give the same items; at their store prices (Design Schematic 2,000, Ship and Module Schematics 10,000) T5 paid 132% (`--sim --store-price`); W11e cut those picks' odds, 116.7-119.7% at every tier (section 19). Each data site's cans count in its value (3 x 1.5 minutes of slicing, 85% success assumed), the locked share of cache sites in the cache sites' value and time, a derelict's data core in the derelict's, and the site ships' lockboxes where slicing them pays. Combat with lockboxes is the trade baseline plus 2% normal-kill lockboxes where slicing them beats killing (T5 +2.4%, 9,380/h; elsewhere none, since they can be kept or sold instead). Cache credits by tier (data site / locked cache): T1 250 / 150, T2 450 / 275, T3 450 / 325, T4 400 / 250, T5 50 / 125, T6 1750 / 1025 (T5 data cans nearly pay their site already); derelict caches move with the core's time and value (T3 625 to 675, T5 150 to 75).
  - **Tests:** `SpaceSlicingTests` (the Data Analyzer, range, who slices, tiers, the rules kept, each source's start and reward roll, the ground tables unchanged, schematic and intel fallbacks, customs outcomes, data core claim, lockbox drops and contents, Espionage XP, the payout) and `ScanningTests` (data sites spawn); engine tests `SpaceSlicingEngineTests` (category SpaceSlicing: a data site's cans and its cache, a locked cache) built, not run.
  - **Bible:** the Data Analyzer's Engineering Recipes row (`bible_recipes.py`), the Scanning (Revamp) tab's data site and locked cache rows and the new Slicing (Revamp) tab (`bible_tabs.py`) are in the workbook.
  - **Droid slicing (review fix 2026-10-30):** a droid at the Sensors seat slices with no mini-game when the pilot has the Slicing rank for the target: its press opens the target at its seat effectiveness (at most 85%, `DroidCrewRules.MaximumSlicingChance`) or loses it, then it works the target for `DroidCrewRules.SlicingSeconds` before the next. The press resolves at once and the time runs while the pilot flies, so a locked cache or a data core costs no slicing time; at 90 s a droid-run explorer (the best industrial droid, Survey Protocol II on the Probe Launcher, no Espionage) paid 128-138% of combat with no crew bonus and up to 146% with Crew Morale, Leadership 50 and the Czerka Armored Transport (`scanning.py --sim`). `SlicingSeconds` is now 120: 126-132% with no crew bonus, 133-140% with every bonus, inside the 100-140% band; a player slicer stays at 114-115%. Sites respawn 5 minutes after completion and an area's round takes 12-15 minutes with the droid, so regeneration never binds. Open: at store prices (`--sim --store-price`) the droid path pays 129-146% against the 120% ceiling; the full fix is to make the droid's time a channel the pilot waits out in range (modelled at 119-129% with schematics as components).
  - **Not built here:** derelict terminals and lockboxes (section 28) and agent data heists (section 29), agent intel itself (W15), placed aces (W9), Wild Space only schematics from Wild Space targets (W12c), a Data Analyzer ladder (one item, no ranks), slicing tools aimed at space boards (the ground tools work on any board of their tier), and client verification of the Data Analyzer, the data cans, the locked cache and the Slice the Scan button.

## 25. Player stations

A station is a **player city in space** and follows every city mechanic, in `Service/Property.cs` and the city layouts:
- a mayor, who needs City Management
- citizens, with one citizenship per player across stations and planet cities
- elections every three weeks
- taxes, weekly upkeep and loss after four weeks unpaid
- city level by citizen count
- upgrades

**Founding**
- A **Station Core**, crafted with Fabrication, takes the place of the City Hall item. It is used at an **anchor site**: 2 per orbit and 1-2 per Wild Space system, one station per site.
- Founding creates two things:
  - the station exterior at the site, using the existing `[mdrn]SpaceStation` placeable scaled by city level
  - the station's own interior deck area, which is the city's land. The City Hall sits inside it.
- Add a station city layout beside `CityLayoutDefinition`, with the same building and structure limits.

**Docking and buildings**
- **Docking:** through the Starport building, as in planet cities: any starport takes Large hulls, and Capital hulls need starport upgrade level 2.
- **Passenger shuttle:** every station's Starport has a flights terminal on the existing shuttle network, with a route to its orbit's NPC dock and the city's transport tax as in planet cities. Today `StarportFlightsDialog` and `Shuttle.TryPurchaseTicket` route between `PlanetType` values, so stations need their own origin and destination entries. Non-pilots can visit, live and roleplay on a station, so stations never become pilot-only hubs.
- **City buildings:** all of them: City Hall, Starport (with the Trade Exchange and its tariff), Medical Center (respawn), Bank, Cantina.
- **Station-only buildings:**
  - **Shipyard:** Capital hull recipes can only be crafted at a Shipyard. A droid assigned to it shortens builds.
    - **Station-only by design (owner decision, 2026-10-30):** there is no NPC Shipyard and no fallback. A player builds a Capital hull only at a station whose Shipyard admits them (as a citizen, or because its mayor opened it to anyone). Capital construction therefore depends on stations building and opening Shipyards; that is intended, so stations matter.
    - **Access:** the mayor decides who can build there: citizens only (the default) or anyone. The mayor can also set an optional Shipyard fee paid into the treasury, added as a new `PropertyTaxType` beside Citizenship and Transportation and edited with the existing `EditTaxes` permission. Several stations can each have a Shipyard, so the first one can't lock out everyone else.
  - **Asteroid Drill:** extracts the anchor site's ore over time into station storage, at the system's tier. An assigned droid raises output.
  - **Jump Beacon:** jumps a docked ship to any other beacon whose city permits it.
    - Each jump costs **Station Fuel**, a trade good.
    - Beacon level caps hull size.
    - Beacons never connect into or out of Wild Space.

**Not in this release**
- Upkeep is city upkeep in credits; there is no other upkeep.
- Stations cannot be attacked. Raids and the Defense Grid come in a later pass.

**Added by the stations update (W13a):**
- **Numbers:** every station number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/stations.py`, which generates `StationTuning` (`Service/StationService`) and the Bible's Player Stations (Revamp) tab (`stations.stations_tab`, listed in `bible_tabs.py`). Draft numbers, tune in testing.
- **A station is a city:** `PropertyType.City` with the new layout `PropertyLayoutType.Station` (115, `StationLayoutDefinition` beside `CityLayoutDefinition`), so citizenship (one per player across stations and planet cities), elections, taxes, the treasury, upkeep and decay, city levels, upgrades, permissions and the Manage City window are the city's code. `WorldProperty.StationAnchorId` (indexed; empty on every other property, so no migration) names its anchor site; its `ParentPropertyId` is the anchor's space area. `Station.IsStation` tells the two kinds apart. Weekly upkeep moved into `CityUpkeepRules` (the same formula for both).
- **Anchor sites (decision on where):** 25 waypoints tagged `STATION_ANCHOR_<ID>`, inserted into the area git JSON by text: two in each orbit with an NPC dock (Viscara, Tatooine, Mon Cala, Hutlar, Dathomir, Korriban, Dantooine, Smuggler's Moon) and one or two per Wild Space system (Vesh Pulsar 2, The Maw 1, Ion Veil 2, Korvax Magnetar 1, Ember Giant 2, Ship Graveyard 1). The Mid Rim systems (no dock) and the Small/Medium zones have none. Each sits 40 m or more from every placeable and waypoint of its area. `StationRules.IsSiteFree` (pure) and `Station.GetStationAtAnchor` (the indexed query) keep one station per site.
- **Founding:** the Station Core (`structure_5018`, `StructureType.StationCore` 5018, the City Hall item's model; `RecipeType.StationCore` 1524, Fabrication 50: 3 Construction Parts, 2 Power Supply Units, 4 Reinforced Construction Parts and 2 Reinforced Power Supply Units, the City Hall's and Starport's recipes together) is used in space within 40 m of a free anchor (`Station.BeginFounding` from `Property.PlaceStructure`; `StationRules.GetFoundingRefusal`: in space, an anchor in range, an area that admits every hull, a free site, no other citizenship, City Management I, 100,000 credits). `PlaceStationCoreDialog` repeats the city terms and checks everything again on confirm. `Property.CreateStation` creates the city, its deck and exterior, the Core building and the station's first Starport, and makes the founder mayor and citizen. Price, upkeep (5,000 a day, charged weekly, -5% per Upkeep perk level, +10,000 a week per Medical Center, Starport and Cantina level above 1), limits (75 structures, 21 buildings), citizens (5-25 for levels 1-5, 18 h grace), the 30 day disrepair deadline and three-week elections are the planet city's.
- **Decision, the first Starport:** docking goes only through a Starport and a station has no other way in, so founding also builds a Starport (the Core's recipe includes it). A station's last Starport cannot be retrieved (`ManageStructuresViewModel`).
- **The deck (decision):** `station_deck` (Module `are`/`git`/`gic`, module area list, the toolset's Building Template category), a copy of Smuggler's Moon's Corporate District (`pw_ar_narscorpd`, 16 x 22 tiles) with its NPCs, doors, transitions, triggers, sounds, stores, waypoints and working placeables removed and its 1,556 static scenery placeables kept, plus a `PROPERTY_ENTRANCE`, flagged interior (no weather) and named as a building template. Each station is an instance of it (`Property.SpawnStationDeck`, the instance registered to the city id so building placement, permissions and the city menus work unchanged); its structures resolve their area to the deck at load. Deck slots (`StationDeckRules`, `StationTuning.DeckSlots`): the Core, Starport, Medical Center, Bank and Cantina snap to their slot, one each per station; the Shipyard, Asteroid Drill, Jump Beacon and Station Fuel slots are kept clear for W13b; every other building and structure keeps 10 m from every slot. Slot points are plazas the source area's NPCs stood on.
- **Exterior:** at load (and on founding) `Station.SpawnExterior` places the "Space Station - Civilian 3" model (`_mdrn_pl_spcs003`, appearance 31478, one of the SWLOR station placeables the orbits use; tag `station_exterior`) 30 m from the anchor toward the area's middle, scaled 1.2 / 1.4 / 1.6 / 1.8 / 2.0 by city level, and a `spc_dock` docking point at the anchor whose `StarportDockDialog` lists only this station's Starports (`Station.RegisterDockPoint`, never a planet's docking menu). Renaming the city renames both.
- **Docking:** a station Starport is a player starport: any hull up to Large docks, Capital hulls need starport level 2 (`StarportDockRequest.IsStationStarport`, refusal `StationStarportTooSmall` 5 with a station message). Ships launch at the anchor (the dockhands' `STARPORT_TELEPORT_WAYPOINT` is the anchor tag). A ship whose station Starport is retrieved and that has no safe dock returns to the station's home port.
- **Exchanges (resolves the W7c deferral):** a station Starport's Trade Exchange (level 2 Starport, the mayor's tariff, as in cities) trades through its anchor's exchange (`StationRules.GetExchange`, `Trade.TryGetCityDock`): its orbit's NPC exchange (the Viscara orbit's second anchor uses CZ-220's), and in Wild Space, which has none, the open port nearest by starlane hops to the orbit the system was charted from (decision, no second market to flood): Vesh Pulsar uses Dathomir, every other system Hutlar.
- **Passenger shuttle:** each anchor has a home port (its orbit's dock; the Wild Space exchange port). A station Starport's flights terminal (open to everyone, since it is the only way off without a ship) flies to its home port, and the home port's terminals list the stations they serve (`ShuttleStop`, `ShuttleRoute`, `ShuttleRouteRules`; `ShuttleRide.OriginStationId`/`DestinationStationId`, empty on older records). An orbit station is an orbital hop (25 credits, 60 s, every 60 s, like CZ-220); a Wild Space station a full flight (1,000 credits, 300 s, every 120 s; decision: passengers only, so it carries no cargo past the rifts). The city's transport tax applies. Passengers arrive at the station's Starport; one offline at arrival is saved at the home port, since a deck is an instance.
- **Medical Center respawn (stations only):** the shared `medical_center` interior gained a Medical Registration Terminal (`cloning_reg`), which planet cities' instances remove when they spawn (`MedicalCenterLayoutDefinition`), so planet-city Medical Centers behave as before. On a station, registering saves `Player.RespawnPropertyId`; `Death.GetRespawnLocation` honors it only for a station's Medical Center (`Station.IsStationInterior`) while it is loaded, respawning at its entrance and keeping the last world registration as the fallback. A planet-city terminal, if one is ever reached, refuses registration.
- **Not attackable:** nothing targets a station.
- **Tests:** `StationTests` (anchor counts, placement and clearance, the Module waypoints, founding refusals, the city mirror, the Core recipe and item, upkeep, the exterior, deck slots and the deck area, docking sizes, exchanges, shuttle routes, fares and schedules, wiring); engine tests `PlayerStationEngineTests` (category PlayerStation: anchors in the module, the exterior, Starport retrieval) built, not run.
- **Not built here (W13b):** the Shipyard (Capital hull crafting and its fee `PropertyTaxType`), the Asteroid Drill, Jump Beacons, Station Fuel and any station-only upgrade; their deck slots are reserved. Also not built: client verification of the anchors, the exterior, the deck and its slots, docking and the shuttle (no client evidence), and lore review of the anchor names.

**Added by the station buildings update (W13b):**
- **Numbers:** in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/stations.py` (generated `StationTuning`: `Buildings`, Shipyard, Drill and beacon numbers, `DrillTables`) and `trade.py` (Station Fuel); the droid percents are W14a's (`StationDroidRules`, `droids.py`). The Player Stations (Revamp) tab gained the buildings, the beacon and the drill income; the Fabrication Recipes tab their three rows. Draft numbers, tune in testing.
- **The buildings (decision: no interiors):** a console at its reserved deck slot (one per station) with an existing scenery model 6 m behind it (`StationBuildings.OnPositionChanged`): Shipyard `StructureType` 5019 (console `engineering_term`, model `swd2_crane001`), Asteroid Drill 5020 (`swlor_0034`, `swd3_mindr01`), Jump Beacon 5021 (`swlor_0024`, `_mdrn_pl_comtwr1`). `City`-only structures without a layout, counted against the building limit, refused off a station deck and (the beacon) in Wild Space (`StationBuildingRules.GetPlacementError`). The console's use script (`stn_bldg_used`) opens `StationBuildingDialog`. Each building's settings, droid and ore are a new `StationBuildingState` record keyed by the structure id (a new type, no migration); removing the building (retrieval or the station's loss, `Property.DeleteProperty`) deletes it and sends an assigned droid to its owner's `/overflow`.
- **Recipes:** Fabrication structures `RecipeType` 1525-1527 after the Station Core (W14a's droids took 7700-7732): Shipyard level 50 (4 Reinforced Construction Parts, 3 Reinforced Power Supply Units, 8 Zinsiam, the Lab's), Asteroid Drill 45 (4, 2), Jump Beacon 48 (4, 3). Items `structure_5019`-`structure_5021` (the Station Core's item copied) in the custom item palette.
- **Shipyard:** the 11 Capital hull deed recipes carry `RecipeShipyardRequirement`: crafted only within 10 m of a Shipyard console (tag `STATION_SHIPYARD`) whose access admits the crafter. Existing Capital hulls and the legacy capital construction components are unaffected. The console opens the Engineering recipes, so any Engineering recipe can be crafted there. Access (the mayor, Edit Taxes permission): citizens only (default; citizens who owe taxes are refused) or anyone. Fee: `PropertyTaxType.Shipyard` 4, flat 0-50,000 credits per Capital hull from everyone who builds (decision: like the citizenship tax, citizens included), edited in Manage City (station only) with the Edit Taxes permission like the exchange tariff, checked with the blueprint cost and paid into the treasury when the build starts. A droid: crafting is the progress mini-game, so "shorter builds" is more progress per synthesis step on Capital deeds (`StationDroidRules.ShipyardBuildSpeedPercent`, +30% at 100 Industry).
- **Asteroid Drill:** 150 ore a day, rolled per unit from the anchor area's asteroid loot tables in the shares its resource spawn table spawns them (the area's spawn waypoint table, each asteroid's `ASTEROID_LOOT_TABLE_ID`), so a drill mines the site's own ore (decision: Smuggler's Moon's orbit spawns Viscara's tier 1-2 rocks, so its drill does too). Accrued lazily when the console is used (`StationBuildingRules.AccrueDrill`): whole units, the remainder carried; storage holds 3 days and a full drill stops and restarts from the moment it is read. Collecting (Access Treasury permission, decision: the ore is the city's) creates the ore on the player until the inventory is full. A droid adds `StationDroidRules.DrillOutputPercent` (+50% at 100 Industry, +70% with Efficiency Protocol II). Income (ore at the 40% NPC store price, an active miner's rate from `mining.py`; W11e's asteroid tables): 855 credits a day in Viscara and Smuggler's Moon orbit, 1,080 Mon Cala, 1,561 Hutlar, 1,821 Tatooine, 2,246 Dathomir and Dantooine, 2,543 Korriban, 2,116 in Wild Space; 0.20-0.25 of that system's mining hour a day, 0.33-0.43 with the best droid (`stations.py` refuses 1.0 or more).
- **Jump Beacon:** the console jumps a ship the player may pilot (`PilotShip`) docked at one of this station's Starports to a Starport of another station whose beacon admits the player. Both beacons' access settings apply (decision: who may jump to and from it): citizens (default), citizens and allies (the stations the mayor lists on the console) or anyone, set with the Edit Taxes permission. Beacon level is a new city upgrade, `PropertyUpgradeType.JumpBeaconLevel` 6 (1-3, Manage City on stations, the usual 50,000 x level price, city level and mayor perk gates, and +10,000 upkeep a week per level above 1); level 1 jumps up to Medium hulls, 2 Large, 3 Capital, and both beacons must take the hull. The destination Starport's W6b rules still apply (a Capital hull needs starport level 2). A jump burns Station Fuel from the ship's cargo bay: Small 1, Medium 2, Large 4, Capital 8 units; the Hutt Grand Barge 50% less, rounded up. Review 2026-10-30: cargo burns more, 1 unit per 6,000 credits of cargo value per starlane hop between the two stations' systems (`StationTuning.JumpFuelCargoValuePerHop`, hops between their anchors' exchanges, `TradeLookup.GetHops`; cargo valued as pirates value it, commodities at base price and sealed freight at 40 a volume, `PirateAmbushRules.GetCargoValue`), rounded up, before the Barge's discount (`StationBuildingRules.GetJumpFuel(JumpRequest)`; the console's confirmation and the refusal show the total). An empty ship burns what it did; a 300-volume freight load jumped 5 hops burns 2 + 10 on a Medium hull. Without it a beacon network out-earned every trade band (section 13, Jump Beacons in the trade model). Tested in `StationBuildingTests` (`AJumpsCargoBurnsFuelByItsValueAndTheHopsSkipped`). It re-registers the ship at the destination Starport, moves its dock and launch point (the destination's anchor), and takes the player to that Starport. No beacon stands in a Wild Space station, and no jump has a Wild Space end.
- **Station Fuel:** commodity 24 (`CommodityType.StationFuel`), base 250 (Reactor Cores 175 plus Tibanna Gas 27, refined, x1.25), volume 2, legal, made at CZ-220 beside its reactor cores and imported nowhere (decision); every exchange trades it, so a station's own Trade Exchange sells it. Holdings' Processing Plants (W14b) add a second source. The trade sim moved slightly: legal 105% of T3 combat (was 103%), every good after risks 155%, Exalted contraband 188%.
- **The Station Fuel slot stays clear (decision):** fuel is cargo burned from the jumping ship's own bay, so it needs no building.
- **Tests:** `StationBuildingTests` (the buildings and slots, placement, recipes and RecipeType order, items and palette, Capital deed gating, Shipyard access and fee, the droid hook, drill tables against the spawn tables and areas, accrual and storage, beacon levels, fuel, access, refusals, the Wild Space ban, upkeep, Station Fuel, wiring); `StationTests` updated for the filled slots; `TradeRiskTests`' convoy check now allows one unit of each pick for rounding.
- **Not built here:** client evidence for any of it (consoles, models and their scale, the dialog, the Manage City rows, crafting at the Shipyard, a jump); a scale or facing for the scenery models; Shipyard and drill droids posted like ship crew (decision: an assigned droid's controller is kept with the building instead, so it never takes the follow slot and needs no carry checks); Station Fuel from holdings (W14b).

## 26. Planetary Holdings

- **Buying:** at a planet's dock, from a Holdings terminal. Up to 3 per account (every character on one public CD key), with credit upkeep.
- **Types:**

| Planet | Holding types (outputs) |
|---|---|
| Viscara | Crystal Mine (Viscaran crystals), Apiary (Veles honey) |
| CZ-220 | Assembly Line (Czerka machine parts, reactor cores) |
| Mon Cala | Kelp Fishery (seafood), Desalination Plant (purified water) |
| Hutlar | Timber Camp (Aracia timber), Byysk Ranch (hides) |
| Tatooine | Moisture Farm (water), Scrap Yard (Jawa salvage) |
| Smuggler's Moon | Spice Lab (moonspice, contraband), Salt Works (neon salt) |
| Korriban | Excavation Dig (Sith relics, contraband) |
| Dathomir | Herb Grove (witch herbs) |
| Dantooine | Grain Farm (grain), Bantha Ranch (bantha milk) |

- **Output:** one batch of the holding's good per day, stored in that dock's warehouse until the owner's ship collects it as cargo.
- **Upgrades** (levels 1-3) raise output and cost goods from other planets, delivered to the holding's dock. For example, a level-3 Moisture Farm needs Czerka machine parts and reactor cores.
- **Production chains:** a holding upgraded with a **Processing Plant** turns its own output plus a delivered input into a refined good worth more than both. Examples:
  - Grain + Water -> Ration Packs
  - Reactor Cores + Tibanna Gas -> **Station Fuel**
  - Viscaran Crystals + Machine Parts -> Focusing Lenses
  - Hides + Witch Herbs -> Medical Supplies

  The full list is drafted with the commodity table.
- **Droid staff:** an assigned industrial droid raises output by its Industry rating (section 27).
- **Events,** rolled every few days per holding:
  - **Bandit raid:** a short instanced ground fight at the holding. Win to keep the stock, lose to lose it.
  - **Breakdown or sandstorm:** output pauses until parts are delivered.
  - **Boom:** double output for a day.
  - **Customs inspection** (contraband holdings only): pay a fine or lose the stock.
- **Ground sites:** each holding type has a small ground area (an instanced copy of a template), reached from its planet's dock. Owners manage the holding and fight its events there.
- **Economy:** new goods follow `EconomyRestrictedItems.md`. Holdings output is cargo (section 13), not items.
- **Added by the holdings update (W14b):**
  - **Numbers:** every holding number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/holdings.py`, which generates `HoldingType`, `HoldingSetbackType`, `HoldingTuning` (`Service/HoldingService`) and `HoldingCatalog` (`Feature/HoldingDefinition`), and the Bible's Planetary Holdings (Revamp) tab (`holdings.holdings_tab`, listed in `bible_tabs.py`). `holdings.py --sim` prints the income table, the chains, the upgrade bills and the event simulation. Draft numbers, tune in testing.
  - **Types:** the 15 above (`HoldingType` 1-15), one good each: Crystal Mine (Viscaran Crystals), Apiary (Veles Honey), Assembly Line (Czerka Machine Parts; decision: one good per holding, so not reactor cores), Kelp Fishery (Seafood), Desalination Plant (Purified Water), Timber Camp (Aracia Timber), Byysk Ranch (Byysk Hides), Moisture Farm (Purified Water), Scrap Yard (Jawa Salvage), Spice Lab (Moonspice), Salt Works (Neon Salt), Excavation Dig (Sith Relics), Herb Grove (Witch Herbs), Grain Farm (Grain), Bantha Ranch (Bantha Milk). Every base output was already a commodity.
  - **Buying:** the Dock Master's new "Planetary Holdings" entry at every NPC planet starport with an exchange (`StarportDialog`; not player-city or station starports, not ship-only locations) opens the Planetary Holdings window (`GuiWindowType.PlanetaryHoldings` 82): a Buy tab with that planet's types and a My Holdings tab. Up to 3 per account since the 2026-10-30 review (`HoldingTuning.MaximumHoldings`; below). A holding is a new entity, `PlanetaryHolding` (indexed `OwnerPlayerId`; a new type, so no migration), tied to the landing area resref of the dock it was bought at (Smuggler's Moon's two docks are separate). Price: 10 days of its level 1 output at base price (8,200 at CZ-220 to 46,900 at Korriban). Abandoning refunds half the price less the upkeep owed.
  - **Output and upkeep:** holdings are passive income and an account may own three, so all three at their best pay at most 90 minutes of the tier's combat rate a day together. A level 1 holding makes a batch worth 12 minutes of its dock tier's space combat rate (T1 4,100 to T6 22,200 an hour, the W7a figures, frozen in `holdings.py`) at base price, once a day, into the dock warehouse (`PlanetaryHolding.Warehouse`), which holds 7 days of output; a full warehouse stops output. Upkeep is 17% (15% before the 2026-10-30 review) of the day's output value at base price, the staff droid's share included (staff wages), owed daily and paid automatically when collecting (or with Pay Upkeep); 14 days owed forecloses the holding (deleted, the droid released, the owner told). Holdings catch up lazily, one whole day at a time, when the owner logs in or opens the window (`HoldingRules.Advance`), so no scheduler runs.
  - **Collection:** Collect loads the warehouse into the cargo bay of the owner's ship docked at that dock (`PlayerShip.Cargo`, as much as fits, the most valuable per volume first), never items. Collected units count as costing their base price, as scooped cargo does, so a sale gives trade XP only for the haul.
  - **Upgrades:** level 2 costs 1.5 days of level 1 output in credits plus 1 day's worth of one good from another planet; level 3 2 days plus 1.5 days' worth of two goods (a level 3 Moisture Farm needs Czerka Machine Parts and Reactor Cores). Goods are delivered from the docked ship's cargo bay into the holding's supplies (the window's Deliveries list, up to what it still takes) and used up by the upgrade. Output x1.0 / 1.25 / 1.45 by level; upkeep scales the same. Without a droid, level 2 pays back in 9-12 days and level 3 in 18-25 days (`holdings.py --sim`, Payback). Decision: upgrades and the Processing Plant are credits plus hauled goods, not Fabrication items (section 14's table), so they need no blueprints, recipes or RecipeType.
  - **Processing Plants:** 2 days of output in credits plus 1 day's worth of a hauled good (pays back in 9-16 days at level 3). Then 35% of each day's output (`HoldingTuning.PlantShare`; half until W11e) runs whole recipes of its chain with delivered input (stocked up to 7 days of recipes; the rest stays raw; a checkbox turns refining off). Every chain's refined batch is worth 1.15-1.23x its two inputs at base price: Crystal Mine 5 crystals + 2 machine parts -> 2 Focusing Lenses; Assembly Line 3 machine parts + 4 crystals -> 2 Focusing Lenses; Apiary 2 honey + 1 witch herbs -> 2 Medical Supplies; Byysk Ranch 4 hides + 3 herbs -> 6 Medical Supplies; Herb Grove 3 herbs + 4 byysk hides -> 6 Medical Supplies; Grain Farm and Moisture Farm grain + water -> Ration Packs; Kelp Fishery 5 seafood + 3 grain -> 3 Ration Packs; Salt Works 5 neon salt + 3 seafood -> 3 Ration Packs; Desalination Plant 4 water + 2 reactor cores -> 3 Station Fuel; Timber Camp 2 timber + 1 machine parts -> 1 Prefab Shelter; Scrap Yard 3 salvage + 1 timber -> 1 Prefab Shelter; Spice Lab moonspice + abdamaryllia -> Glitterstim; Excavation Dig 1 relic + 2 encrypted data chips -> 1 Sith Holocron; Bantha Ranch 2 milk + 1 neon salt -> 1 Aged Bantha Cheese.
  - **Station Fuel's second source (decision):** the Desalination Plant's chain makes Station Fuel (W13b's CZ-220 export) from water cracked with reactor power, in place of the plan's Reactor Cores + Tibanna Gas: Tibanna Gas is a crafting material, not a commodity, and Reactor Cores are CZ-220's own export, so no holding there could use them as a hauled input.
  - **Holding goods (section 13):** six new commodities, `CommodityType` 25-30 after W13b's Station Fuel (24): Ration Packs (150, volume 2), Focusing Lenses (200, 1), Prefab Shelters (270, 4), Glitterstim (600, 1, contraband), Sith Holocrons (1,300, 2, restricted by the Republic) and Aged Bantha Cheese (215, 2), in `trade.py` (`HOLDING_GOODS`, `HOLDING_GOOD_SOURCES`, `HOLDING_GOOD_CONSUMERS`) and the generated `TradeCatalog`. No exchange sells them (`CommodityDetail.HoldingSources`, `IsHoldingGood`; a buy is refused with `TradeRefusal.HoldingsOnly` 9 and the exchange window shows no buy price): exchanges buy them as importers where listed and as neutral markets elsewhere, priced by starlane hops from the docks whose holdings refine them. Convoys never carry them and the HoloNet board lists them as "Holdings only". The greedy trader of `trade.py --sim` cannot buy them, so the trade outcomes and `TradeRiskReference.json` are unchanged.
  - **Droid staff:** the window's Droid row posts one of the owner's free droids as staff (`PlanetaryHoldings.AssignDroid`), like ship droid crew: the controller stays with the owner, cursed and marked posted (`DROID_POSTED_SHIP` = `HOLDING:<id>`, `HoldingRules.DroidPostingPrefix`), never spawned, so it takes no follow slot. Output rises by `DroidIndustryRules.HoldingOutputPercent` (+0.5% per Industry, Efficiency Protocol applies: +25% at Industry 50, +70% at 100 with rank II), read when posted. Recall gives it back. The ship droid crew's login and unacquire checks skip holding postings; the holdings service has its own (a lost controller ends the posting; a holding that no longer lists the droid frees it at login), and the droid controller refuses to summon a holding's droid.
  - **Events:** one roll every 3 days per holding while no setback or fine is pending (`HoldingRules.RollEvent`): boom 12% (that day's output doubles), setback 5% (Breakdown, Blight, Sandstorm, Cave-in, Storm or Herd sickness by type: output stops until its repair goods arrive, half a day's output worth of Czerka Machine Parts, Medical Supplies, or for the Assembly Line Jawa Salvage), bandit raid 5% (half of every good in the warehouse is taken; decision: no ground fight yet), and for the Spice Lab and Excavation Dig only, customs inspection 20% (a fine of 50% of the banned stock's base value, as at a customs patrol, paid in the window within 2 days, or the banned stock is confiscated). Each event goes to the holding's log (the last 8, shown in the window) and to the owner when online. An owner who collects weekly keeps about 93% of the event-free output (84% for the two inspected holdings).
  - **Ground sites:** each type has a site office, an instance of an existing building-template interior (already an instance template, so it is kept out of saved locations): Crystal Mine and Excavation Dig `house_int_7`, Apiary, Byysk Ranch and Herb Grove `house_int_1`, Assembly Line, Desalination Plant, Scrap Yard and Spice Lab `player_rnd`, Kelp Fishery, Moisture Farm and Grain Farm `house_int_4`, Timber Camp, Salt Works and Bantha Ranch `house_int_10`. Visit Site (at the holding's own dock, owner only) makes the holding's instance on first visit and removes it once empty; the template's exit placeable becomes the Holding Office Terminal (`HoldingSiteDialog`: manage the holding, or return to the dock). Decision: no Module changes and no new areas.
  - **Income** (`holdings.py --sim`; daily output at base price less upkeep, in minutes of the dock tier's rate, per holding): level 1 10-11 minutes, level 2 12-14, level 3 14-16, level 3 with an Industry 50 droid 17-19, level 3 with the best droid (Industry 100, Efficiency Protocol II) 24-26, and with a Processing Plant (input bought at base price) 27-30. An account with three holdings averages 30 minutes a day at level 1, 44 at level 3, 74 with the best droids and 84 with Processing Plants (86 before the 2026-10-30 review, 91 before W11e).
  - **Tests:** `PlanetaryHoldingTests` (the catalog against this section, goods from other planets, chain premiums and the Station Fuel source, site templates, buying and the limit, refunds, daily output, upkeep and foreclosure, the full warehouse, collection into cargo, upgrades and deliveries, chains, the droid bonus and posting ids, every event, the log, holding goods at the exchanges, the income check, wiring), `TradeTests` (holding goods have holding sources instead of producers).
  - **Not built here:** the bandit raid's instanced ground fight (it needs raider creatures per planet and an encounter in the sites), real outdoor sites (fields, mines, ranches; area design), holdings at player-city or station starports, guests at a site, refreshing a posted droid's Industry when its parts change (recall and post it again), and client verification of the window, the Dock Master entry, the sites and the staff droid (no client evidence). W11o built the first four (below).
  - **Added by the holdings sites update (W11o):** numbers in `holdings.py` (`RAID_ANSWER_DAYS`, `RAIDERS_PER_RAID`, `RAID_ENTRANCE_CLEAR_METERS`, `RAIDER_ROSTERS`, `OUTDOOR_SITES`; generated `HoldingTuning` and `HoldingCatalog`), on the Planetary Holdings (Revamp) tab (a raids and sites table, the events rule and the income table's defended column).
    - **Bandit raid ground fight:** a raid no longer takes the stock at once. The raiders hold the site (`PlanetaryHolding.RaidPending`/`RaidDeadline`, false on older records, no migration); the window says so with the deadline. Visiting the site spawns 4 raiders of the dock's orbit tier 8 m or more from the entrance (once per instance; existing Normal humanoid ground creatures of the tier's ground level band with their own loot and no quest credit: T1 Viscara outlaws, T2 Mandalorian rangers, hunters and warriors, T3 eco-terrorists, T4 Nar Shaddaa pirates, thieves and blades, T5 troublemakers, Dantari hunters and Kwi tribals, T6 Sith temple guards). The last raider down drives the raid off (`HoldingRules.RepelRaid`: the warehouse is safe). An undefended raid takes half the warehouse when its 2-day window runs out (`HoldingRules.LoseRaid`, the customs window), as every raid did before; no other event rolls meanwhile. Dying only means trying again before the deadline. Income: an owner who visits weekly keeps 91.9% of the event-free output on average with undefended raids and 93.5% defending every raid; three holdings at their best 90 minutes a day, 81 after events and 83 with every raid driven off, inside the 90-minute passive cap (the three best types; 88, 80 and 81 since the 2026-10-30 review).
    - **Outdoor sites (decision: four biome templates):** `holding_plains` (Grain Farm, Bantha Ranch, Byysk Ranch), `holding_forest` (Apiary, Timber Camp, Herb Grove), `holding_desert` (Moisture Farm, Scrap Yard, Salt Works) and `holding_cave` (Crystal Mine, Excavation Dig): stripped copies of Dantooine - Wild Plains, Viscara - Crossroads West, Tatooine - North East Dunes and the Dantooine Crystal Cave prefab (static scenery only, no creatures, doors, triggers, sounds, stores or locals), each with one `PROPERTY_ENTRANCE` where a source creature or landing waypoint stood and the Holding Office Terminal (`building_exit`) 1.5 m beside it (the cave's in its old doorway). Module area list and the toolset's Building Template category; kept out of saved locations (`Area.RemoveInstancesFromCache`). The Assembly Line, Desalination Plant, Spice Lab and Kelp Fishery keep their interiors.
    - **Holdings at player-city and station starports:** the Dock Master's Planetary Holdings entry at a city Starport sells its planet's holdings, at an orbit station's Starport its anchor exchange's (`HoldingRules.GetCityDockHoldingsExchange`; a Wild Space station none). The holding's dock is the Starport (its property id): ships docked there collect and deliver, Visit Site leaves from it. A holding whose Starport is gone (retrieved, the city lost) moves to its exchange's NPC dock the next time its owner opens that dock's holdings (`PlanetaryHoldings.RehomeStrandedHoldings`), so it is never stranded.
    - **Guests:** a member of the owner's party at the holding's dock gets "Join <owner>'s <holding>" from the Dock Master while the owner has the site open (`HoldingRules.CanJoinAsGuest`, `PlanetaryHoldings.JoinSite`); guests fight raids and leave by the terminal, which manages the holding only for its owner.
    - **Tests:** `PlanetaryHoldingTests` (the raid window, loss and repelling, rosters and their loot, the outdoor templates and their wiring, guests, city and station docks). No client evidence: the four sites (walkable entrances, terminal reach, raider spawn spots), the raid fight, the guest entry and the city and station holdings are unverified in a client.
- **Per-account limit and the passive cap (review 2026-10-30):**
  - **Per account:** the limit of 3 counted one character (`LoadHoldings` for one player id), so every extra character multiplied the passive income. A holding now records its owner's public CD key (`PlanetaryHolding.OwnerCDKey`, indexed; older records are filled when their owner's holdings next load, no migration since holdings have not shipped), and a purchase counts every holding on the account (`PlanetaryHoldings.CountAccountHoldings`, `HoldingRules.CountAccountHoldings`: the buyer's own plus the records under the same key of characters that still exist; a deleted character's holdings stop counting), as the quest contract board limits contracts per CD key. Past the limit the purchase is refused: "Your account already owns 3 holdings across its characters, the most an account may own." The window shows "Your account owns N of 3 holdings (M on this character)."
  - **Passive cap:** three holdings at their best (level 3, best droid, Processing Plant, input at base price), event-free, netted 91.8 minutes of the tier's combat a day for three Desalination Plants and 90.3 for the three best types, over the 90-minute cap. Upkeep is now 17% of the day's output value (`holdings.UPKEEP_SHARE`, was 15%): 90.0 and 88.5 (83.7 and 79.7 after events). The Processing Plant share could not do it alone (33% put a plant's payback at 34 days, over the 28-day check). `holdings.py` checks both trios against `MAX_ACCOUNT_MINUTES` (90; `HoldingTuning.MaximumAccountMinutes`); level 1 still nets 10-11 minutes and upgrades pay back in 9-25 days.
  - **City revamp:** the city revamp's farming cap does not include holdings: holdings are capped here, 3 per account and at most 90 minutes a day at their best. Its Industrial Charter edict (+10% output for citizens' holdings on the city's planet) would take three of the best type to about 100 minutes, so it needs its own room under this cap when it is built.
  - **Tests:** `PlanetaryHoldingTests` (`Buying_TheLimitIsPerAccount`, `Income_AnAccountsBestThreeHoldingsStayWithinThePassiveCap`). No client evidence: the window's account line and the refusal are unverified in a client.

## 27. Industrial droids

- **Industrial parts:** every part slot (CPU, Head, Body, Arms, Legs) gets an industrial variant at every tier.
  - Industrial parts give a new **Industry** rating, through a new `DroidStatSubType.Industry` read in `Droid.LoadDroidItemPropertyDetails`.
  - In exchange, they have lower HP and attributes and **no combat skill bonuses**.
  - Examples: Labor Arms, Hauler Legs, Survey Head, Logistics CPU.
  - They are Engineering recipes at each Droid Assembly tier and go in the Bible's Equipment - Droids tab.
- **Cost:** industrial parts cost about half the materials of combat parts at the same tier, so a crew droid is affordable while a top-tier one is still an investment.
- **Industry rating:** a droid's Industry is the sum of its parts. Mixing combat and industrial parts gives a middling droid at both.
- **Industry pays off in five places:**
  - **Planetary Holdings:** an output boost.
  - **Ship droid crew** (section 10): station effectiveness comes from Industry, so a combat droid makes poor crew.
  - **Droid bays:** Mining Barge, Industrial and Logistics hulls get one, using the unused `ShipDetail.HasDroidBay`. A droid riding in the bay boosts mining, gas and salvage yield, or Logistics repair output.
  - **Station buildings:** faster Shipyard builds, more Asteroid Drill output.
  - **Derelict salvage teams** (section 28): a faster timer and a better yield.
- **Droid limit:** working droids don't count toward the one-droid limit (section 10).
- **New AI instructions,** which compete with combat instructions for AI slots:
  - **Efficiency Protocol:** holding, drill and droid-bay output.
  - **Damage Control Protocol:** Engineer station and Logistics bay repairs.
  - **Survey Protocol:** scan strength at the Sensors station.
  - **Prospecting Protocol:** the rare-find chance on mining and salvage.
- **Added by the industrial droids update (W14a):**
  - **Numbers:** every industrial droid number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/droids.py`, which generates `DroidIndustryTuning` (`Service/DroidService`), the part recipes (`DroidIndustrialPartRecipes`), the 25 part and 8 disc blueprints and their custom palette entries, and the Bible's Industrial Droids (Revamp) tab (`droids_tab`, wired into `bible_tabs.py`) and recipe rows (wired into `bible_recipes.py`). Draft numbers, tune in testing. `droids.py --report` prints the bills and the effectiveness table.
  - **Industrial parts:** one per slot per tier, 25 in all: Logistics CPU, Survey Head, Utility Body, Labor Arms and Hauler Legs I-V (`d_in_cpu1`-`5`, `d_in_hd1`-`5`, `d_in_bd1`-`5`, `d_in_ar1`-`5`, `d_in_lg1`-`5`). Engineering recipes in the droid part categories at level 10 x tier - 5, `RecipeType` 7700-7724 (after W13a's possible additions; `DroidIndustryTuning.FirstRecipeType`). Each takes the slot's tier intermediate (CPU unit, droid brain, chassis, manipulator arm, motive system) plus half of one intermediate's raw bill rounded down: 40% (tier 1) to 49% (tier 5) of a combat part's raw materials. Stats: a Logistics CPU has 50 HP (combat 70), every attribute at 10 (combat CPUs have one at 11), no weapon skills and no Armor; the other parts give one attribute at about a third of the combat part's total (Head PER, Body VIT, Arms MGT, Legs AGI) and the combat part's AI slots, so protocols compete with combat instructions for the same slots. Art: each reuses its slot's existing droid part model and icon (CPU model 71); ESS9 has no models left and these simple models take no tint.
  - **Industry:** `DroidStatSubType.Industry` (25; `iprp_droidstat` row 25, TLK 82973 "Industry"). Each industrial part carries 4 x its tier; a droid's Industry is the sum (`Droid.LoadDroidItemPropertyDetails`), so five tier-5 parts give 100 and combat parts add none: a mixed droid is middling at both. The Droid Assembly window shows it (in the old spacer of its third stat column) and writes it on the controller, whose examine lists it with the other droid stats.
  - **Crew effectiveness (section 10):** station effectiveness now comes from Industry, not tier: 25% + (seat best - 25%) x Industry / 100 (`DroidCrewRules.BaseEffectiveness(seat, industry)`; `SpaceCrew.GetDroidIndustryBonus` is the Industry term). The best industrial droid works an Engineer or Sensors seat at 75% and a Gunner seat at 50% (the W0 numbers, so `checks.py` is unchanged); tier 1-5 all-industrial droids work 35/45/55/65/75% (Gunner 30-50%); any combat droid works at 25%, its tier setting only the module ranks it can switch on (tier x 10 - 5, unchanged). Crew Morale and Leadership 50 put the best droid at 86% and the 105% cap still applies to every seat. `ShipDroidCrew` saves `Industry` and the droid's protocols when it is posted (postings from before read 0).
  - **Protocols (new AI instructions):** droid-only perks (`PerkGroupType.Droid`, category `DroidProtocols` 69, never offered to players or beasts), PerkType 1056-1059, `iprp_droidperk` rows 1056-1059 (TLK 82974-82976 and 193150), uploaded from instruction discs `id_efficiency1`-`2`, `id_dmgcontrol1`-`2`, `id_survey1`-`2`, `id_prospect1`-`2` (recipes `RecipeType` 7725-7732 in `DroidInstructionRecipes`, rank I at the tier 1 disc bill and level 10, rank II at the tier 3 bill and level 30). Rank I fits any droid for 1 AI slot; rank II a tier 3+ droid for 2 slots. Built with a consumer now:
    - **Survey Protocol** (+20% / +40% scan strength, `SpaceCrew.GetDroidSurveyBonusPercent`): a droid running it switches on a Probe Launcher in its station's slots whenever something in the area is short of 100% for the ship (`DroidCrewTask.Survey`, `Space.HasSomethingToScan`). A tier-5 best droid with rank II scans at 0.75 x 1.40 = 1.05 against a rank-45 player's 1.45 (72%).
    - **Damage Control Protocol** (+5% / +10% repairs): repairers the droid runs at the Engineer station, and a Logistics hull's bay droid (`Space.GetDroidRepairBonusPercent`, read in `ShipModuleActions.ApplyRepairEfficiency`).
    - **Efficiency Protocol** (+20% / +40% Industry for output): droid-bay yields now; holdings and drills read it through the hooks below.
    - **Prospecting Protocol** (+5 / +10 rare-find points, Deep Core's scale): mining and salvage from a droid bay (`Space.GetDroidBayRareFindBonus`).
  - **Droid bays:** Mining Barge, Industrial and Logistics hulls get one (`ShipDetail.HasDroidBay`, set by `ShipBuilder.DroidBay` from `DroidIndustryRules.HasDroidBay`). It is a droid-only post (`ShipCrewSeat.DroidBay`, never a player seat) posted and recalled from the crew console's "Droid crew" page like a station, saved and seated with the station droids, and it holds one droid. On a Mining Barge or Industrial hull the bay droid adds Industry x 0.25% (raised by Efficiency Protocol: +25% at 100, +35% with rank II) to every mining, strip-mining, gas and salvage cycle's delivered yield (rounded half up, never below the cycle's own; what the asteroid or cloud loses is unchanged). On a Logistics hull it adds Industry x 0.10% plus Damage Control Protocol to every repair the ship makes (+10% at 100, +20% with rank II). The hulls without stations (the Medium Mining Barges and Logistics) open the crew dialog from the docked ship's computer for a captain carrying a droid.
  - **Droid limit:** posted droids (station or bay) are never spawned, so they never take the one follow slot (`DroidCrewRules.CountsTowardFollowLimit`); holdings and station buildings will post droids the same way.
  - **Hooks for later slices** (draft numbers in `droids.py`): `DroidIndustryRules.HoldingOutputPercent` (W14b, +0.5% per Industry, Efficiency applies), `SalvageTeamSpeedPercent` and `SalvageTeamYieldPercent` (W15 derelict salvage teams, +0.3% and +0.25%); station buildings in `Service/StationService/StationDroidRules` for W13b to fill: `TakesDroid` (the Shipyard and Asteroid Drill deck slots W13a reserves), `ShipyardBuildSpeedPercent` (+0.3%) and `DrillOutputPercent` (+0.5%, Efficiency applies).
  - **Tests:** `DroidIndustryTests` (parts, bills, Industry sum and mixing, protocols and their slots, bays, hooks, the follow limit, the recipe ID block), `SpaceDroidCrewTests` (effectiveness from Industry, the 75% rule and the crew cap), `ScanningTests` (the Survey bonus).
  - **Not built here:** holdings, station buildings and derelict salvage teams (their hooks only), refreshing a posted droid's Industry when its parts change (repost it), and client verification of the parts, the Droid Assembly window and the bays.

## 28. Derelict boarding

- **Finding them:** derelicts come from scanning (section 24) and Wild Space.
- **Boarding:** a Small or Medium ship docks with the derelict and the party boards on foot, reusing the ship-to-ship docking flow (section 6, "Added by the docking update").
- **Inside:** an instanced copy of a derelict template: existing starship interior layouts dressed as wrecks, plus a few new ones. It is a ground dungeon at the system's tier, with ground enemies, ground loot, slicing terminals and lockboxes at the system's tier, and ground abilities. Each mode keeps its own abilities.
- **Safety:** the docked ship can't be attacked while its crew is aboard the derelict. Leaving returns everyone to the ship.
- **Contested derelicts:** some derelicts are contested.
  - While the boarding party is inside, enemy fighters attack in waves around the derelict, and pilots who stay outside defend it. The docked ship stays safe.
  - Each wave cleared raises the boarders' loot.
  - If no defending ship is left, enemy boarders reinforce the derelict's interior.
  - It reuses combat-site waves (section 24) and the derelict dungeon, and gives a mixed space and ground group one shared goal.
- **Droid salvage teams:** instead of boarding, a docked ship can send its working droids (station crew or droid-bay droids, section 27) to strip the derelict on a timer while the pilot holds off waves outside, using the contested-derelict waves. The droids' Industry rating sets the timer and the yield. It pays less than boarding on foot and gives solo pilots and small groups a way in.
- **Added by the derelict boarding update (W15c):**
  - **Numbers:** every derelict number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/derelicts.py`, which generates `DerelictTuning` (`Service/SpaceService`) and the Bible's Derelict Boarding (Revamp) tab (`derelicts_tab`, wired into `bible_tabs.py`; the workbook is not regenerated here). `derelicts.py --sim` prints the payouts. Draft numbers, tune in testing. Content tiers: the space tiers 1-6 and Wild Space as index 7 (tier 5.5); space tier 6 uses the ground's 5.5 band, and slicing terminals and lockboxes stop at tier 5, as space slicing does.
  - **Finding (decision):** 50% of derelict signatures are boardable and 40% of those are contested (`DerelictRules.RollsBoardable`/`RollsContested`, rolled when the signature spawns, `ScanSignature.Boardable`/`Contested`), in the orbits, zones and Wild Space alike; faction agent leads never are. The Scanner tab's tooltip says so from the threat level, and a boardable derelict's wreck is named "Boardable Derelict" (it now carries `SCAN_SIGNATURE_ID` and lives the site lifetime plus the boarding hold; ending the site removes it as before).
  - **Boarding (decision: a dedicated `/board`, on the W8c flow):** the pilot targets the derelict's wreck and types `/board` (`Space.BoardDerelict`, `SpaceDerelicts.cs`). `DerelictRules.GetBoardRefusal` checks, in order: a derelict, boardable, not yet stripped, the pilot's party at 100% on its signature, a Small or Medium hull, within 30 m (`ShipDockingRules.DockRange`), out of combat (`IsShipInCombat`), its defenses destroyed, no salvage team on it, the party that boarded first, and room (2 ships). The ship docks as at a host: it loses its current position and its pilot leaves space mode, so it is not in space and cannot be attacked; its viewport watchers, station crew and interior passengers board with the pilot. `PlayerShip.DockedDerelictId` marks it (loads empty, no migration; a mark left by a restart is cleared when read, and the ship is at its last dock). Ship Management shows it docked aboard the derelict (`IsDockedAboardHost` covers it) and does not let it be boarded from a starport.
  - **Inside (decision: existing interiors reused unchanged as templates, dressed when instanced):** an instance (`Area.CreateInstance`) of `ship_merchant_z` "Derelict Merchant Freighter", `ship_throne_z` "Derelict Throne Yacht" or `ship_consul_z` "Derelict Consular Cruiser": its ship computer goes dead, its exit becomes the Airlock, and 10 debris pieces (`swd2_debr001`, `swd3_dbpil02`, `swd_jkpl001`, `swd_jkpl002`, `zep_smokesm`), 8 ground enemies, 2 slicing terminals (`slice_term_1`-`5`, the ground terminals and rewards) and the Derelict Strongbox (the site cache crate `spc_sig_cache`, at the farthest spot found) go at baked walkmesh points 8 m or more from the entrance. No new areas, so no module repack. The enemies are existing Normal ground creatures of the level band with their own loot (the spawn system's scripts and stats; their `QUEST_NPC_GROUP_ID` is removed, so the borrowed creatures give no dungeon quest credit): T1 CZ-220 mynocks and malfunctioning droids, T2 Mandalorian salvagers and Vellen flesheaters, T3 the Abandoned Station's reanimated crew and colicoids, T4 Nar Shaddaa's scavenger droids and pirates, T5 the Hutlar Droid Foundry's intake and assembly droids, T6 and Wild Space its core droids (war droid, siege droid, hunter-killer). Ground abilities work: it is a ground area.
  - **Strongbox:** its maglock holds until every enemy aboard is down (reinforcements included). Opening it pays each boarder aboard who fought (on the enmity table of an enemy that died aboard, or its killer: `DerelictBoarding.Participants`) and the opener, never idle passengers or DMs, `DerelictTuning.StrongboxCredits` (T1-T6 475 / 750 / 1,200 / 1,175 / 1,050 / 3,025, Wild Space 1,025) and 1 ground lockbox of the slicing tier (`lockbox_t1`-`5`), and each party ship flying within 60 m half of the credits. The derelict is then stripped (`DerelictLooted`) and cannot be boarded or salvaged again.
  - **Leaving and safety:** the Airlock takes a boarder back to the ship they came with (otherwise one they own, otherwise the first docked; `DerelictRules.ChooseShip`), and the ship's own exit offers "Cross to the <derelict>" while it is docked. Undocking is the ship's computer or `/undock` aboard (`Space.UndockFromDerelict`): the pilot launches 10 m beside the derelict (`ShipDockingRules.GetUndockOffset`), and on arrival (`PendingShipLaunch.UndockFromDerelictId`) the ship leaves the derelict and the rest of its crew still aboard come back to its interior. A boarding ends when no ship is docked and nobody is aboard, or after its 40-minute hold (`BoardingHoldMinutes`; the site stays open meanwhile through `ScanSignature.HeldUntil`). A ship still docked then goes into space beside the derelict when anyone is aboard it or the derelict, otherwise back to its last dock; boarders still aboard go to their ship's interior, otherwise to the dock it is towed to (the escape-pod destination, no penalty), otherwise the start. A boarder who logs out aboard comes back to the airlock while the boarding lasts (`PersistentLocation.LoadLocation`, `Space.TryGetDerelictLoginRedirect`). Derelicts live in memory: a restart ends them and the boot returns every ship to its dock.
  - **Contested derelicts:** while a ship of the party is docked, waves of the tier's combat-site squad (`SignatureSiteRules.CombatSquad`, Rift Raiders in Wild Space) arrive on a 25 m ring around the derelict: the first 30 s after boarding, the next 30 s after each is destroyed, 3 at most. Each wave cleared before the strongbox opens raises its credits by the tier's step (T1-T6 20 / 20 / 5 / 5 / 35 / 30%, Wild Space 60%, after W11e and the master merge's loot baseline; solved in `derelicts.py` so the mixed group earns 130% of combat, held to 5-60% since W11e (10-60% held T3-T4 at 138-142%)) and adds a lockbox. While a wave is out, someone is aboard and no player ship flies within 60 m, 2 enemy boarders of the tier breach at the airlock every 60 s (6 at most), and they must die before the strongbox opens.
  - **Droid salvage teams (decision: the ship does not dock, it holds beside the derelict):** `/salvage` on a boardable derelict (`Space.SendSalvageTeam`; `DerelictRules.GetSalvageRefusal`: any hull with a posted droid, in range, out of combat, defenses cleared, nobody boarding it) sends the droids posted at the ship's stations and droid bay, who leave their posts until the team is back. The team's Industry is its droids' summed, capped at 100. The timer is 480 s shortened by `DroidIndustryRules.SalvageTeamSpeedPercent` (387 s at Industry 80, 369 s at 100); the team brings back `SalvageCredits` (T1-T6 225 / 400 / 525 / 425 / 525 / 1,400, Wild Space 625) and 6 Ship Scrap, both raised by `SalvageTeamYieldPercent`, and one pull from the site's elite table into the hold. The ship must stay within 60 m: leaving, docking, logging out or dying calls the team back empty-handed. On a contested derelict the same waves come and the droids take cover while one is out (the timer pauses), so the pilot holds them off.
  - **Payout (`derelicts.py --sim`; the tools have no ground dungeon rate, so the tier's space combat rate is the proxy):** boarding (9.25 minutes aboard: 8 ground fights of 0.75 min, 2 min of walking, docking and undocking; terminals and lockboxes pay the ground slicing rewards and are left out, as rare drops are) pays 114.4-116.3% of combat at every tier (T3 9,795/h against 8,500). Contested, one boarder and one defender: 127.4-141.5% of combat per player (T3 11,687/h, 119% of boarding). Salvage teams, solo, uncontested: 64.5-66.2% of boarding at Industry 80 (45-47% at 0, 70-72% at 100); contested: 64-89% of the contested group's rate.
  - **Tests:** `DerelictBoardingTests` (the shares, the Scanner line, the board and salvage gates, the commands, tiers and rosters, the interior plan and every blueprint, the templates, the hold, safety, returning, abandonment, waves, the loot step, the strongbox gate, reinforcements, radii, the salvage timer, yield and pause, the payout bands) and the engine test `DerelictEngineTests` (category Derelict: each template's interior; built, not run).
  - **Not built here:** new derelict interiors (the "few new ones"), hostile boarding, and client evidence: docking, the interiors and their spawn spots, the waves, the breach and the salvage team are unverified in a client.
  - **Added by the follow-ups update (W11b):** the Ship HUD's Scanner tab has Board and Salvage buttons next to Jump. They work on a boardable derelict that is not stripped and that the party can jump to (`ScanningRules.CanBoard`, `ScannerRow.CanBoard`); a click targets the signature's wreck in the pilot's area (`Space.TargetDerelictSignature`) and runs `/board` or `/salvage`, whose gates say why anything else fails. Jump narrowed to 52 px to make room. Tests: `DerelictBoardingTests`. No client evidence.
  - **Added by the derelict interiors update (W11o):** four more hull layouts are derelict templates (`derelicts.py` `TEMPLATES`, generated `DerelictTuning`), dressed at runtime the same way: `ship_mule_z` "Derelict Mule Hauler", `ship_falchion_z` "Derelict Falchion Runner", `ship_saber_z` "Derelict Saber Prospector" and `ship_corv_v` "Derelict Corvette" (its crew console goes dead too). Decision: the roomy layouts only, each with 27 or more placeables 10 m or more from the entrance for the placement fallback; the cramped Small layouts (Panther, Hound, Condor, Striker, Fighter, Basilisk, Light Freighter) stay out. No new areas, payouts unchanged. Tests: `DerelictBoardingTests`; the engine test `DerelictEngineTests` covers every template (built, not run). No client evidence.

## 29. Faction agents

- **Placement:** two agents per faction at different docks, with no dock hosting more than two. Each faction's first dock is the lower-tier one.

| Faction | First dock | Second dock |
|---|---|---|
| Republic Military | Veles (Viscara) | Dantooine |
| Czerka | CZ-220 | Hutlar |
| Smugglers | Mon Cala | Smuggler's Moon Station |
| Hutt Cartel | Anchorhead (Tatooine) | Smuggler's Moon Landing |
| Mandalorian | Hutlar | Dathomir |
| Sith Military | Korriban | Dathomir |

- **Missions:** kill, sealed courier, mining quota, convoy escort, scan-down and data heist (slice a target data can inside an enemy-held site), scaled to the dock's tier. Higher standing unlocks harder missions.
- **Rewards:**
  - credits
  - faction standing (the faction service)
  - **faction tokens,** one currency per faction, spent at that faction's store on Design Schematics, ammo variants, components and interior trophies
- **Implementation:** missions are runtime quests (`Quest.RegisterRuntimeQuest`).
- **Added by the faction agents update (W15a):**
  - **Numbers:** every agent number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/agents.py`, which generates `FactionAgentTuning` (`Service/FactionAgentService`), the test reference `SWLOR.Game.Server.Tests/Fixtures/FactionAgents/FactionAgentReference.json` and the Bible's Faction Agents (Revamp) tab (`agents_tab`, wired into `bible_tabs.py`). `agents.py --sim` prints the payout against combat. Draft numbers, tune in testing.
  - **Agents** (`FactionAgentType` 1-12, `FactionAgentDetail`): Lieutenant Corin Valse (Republic Military, Veles) and Commander Neela Tarsk (Dantooine); Requisitions Officer Mira Kell (Czerka, CZ-220) and Contracts Manager Dorn Halvek (Hutlar); Teeso Vrill (Smugglers, Mon Cala) and Kesha Doon (Smuggler's Moon Station); Bargo Ruul (Hutt Cartel, Anchorhead) and Vex Marrock (Smuggler's Moon Landing); Kast Varad (Mandalorian, Hutlar) and Kaldren Varos (Dathomir); Lieutenant Vael Moraq (Sith Military, Dathomir) and Captain Seris Drask (Korriban). Hutlar and Dathomir host two each, every other dock one. Decision: the table lists Korriban first for the Sith, but Dathomir (tier 5) is the lower tier, so it is the Sith's first dock. A dock's tier is its exchange's (`TradeExchangeDetail.OrbitTier`).
  - **Placement** (Module `git`, needs a module repack): each agent is a placed creature in its dock's landing area (the Dock Master's area, the dock id `Trade` uses), copied from a placed instance of an existing faction NPC blueprint (Republic Officer, Female Republic Soldier, Czerka employees, Rodian smugglers, Gran and human bounty hunters, Mandalorian, Sith troopers) with that instance's equipment, Plot, the Dock Master's scripts and the locals `CONVERSATION=FactionAgentDialog` and `FACTION_AGENT_ID`. Each stands 1 m to the Dock Master's right (the second at a dock 1 m to its left), facing the same way. **Unverified in a client:** every spot needs a walkmesh check and may need moving in the toolset.
  - **The board** (`FactionAgentRules.GetBoard`, `FactionAgentDialog`): each agent offers the same board to everyone, replaced at 00:00 UTC: two standard offers at the dock's tier (Neutral or better), one priority offer a tier higher (at most 6, Trusted 250 or better) and an intel lead (a data heist a tier higher, Neutral, spends one agent intel). No mission type repeats on a board; each faction weights the types (Czerka favors mining, Smugglers couriers and heists, Mandalorians kills). An offer can be taken once (`Player.AgentOffersTaken`, today's and yesterday's ids); a player holds at most 3 missions across every agent. A Hostile pilot (-500 or lower) gets no missions and no store.
  - **Missions** (`AgentMissionType`, `AgentMission` on `Player.AgentMissions` with its terms; the quest is rebuilt at login, so it survives a restart; older records load empty, no migration). One quest state; a counter (`CombatObjective`, key `agent:<id>`) that completes and pays the moment the mission is done, except the mining quota, which is handed in to its agent (`CollectItemObjective`). Abandoning (the agent, the journal, or a courier's Freight tab) ends it with what it set up.
    - **Kill:** 10 ships of the tier, or 8 fighters, 6 interceptors or 3 gunships, in a named system whose spawn tables fly that tier (Smuggler's Moon tier 1, Mid Rim 1 tier 5, Mid Rim 2 tier 6), the nearest to the dock or one jump further. Kills by the killer's party in the system count (`OnCreatureDeathBefore`); lawful convoy and customs ships never do (`Space.TryGetMissionKill`).
    - **Sealed courier:** W7b sealed freight: on accepting, a load of 10 / 10 / 20 / 20 / 40 / 40 volume (by tier) goes into one of the player's ships docked at the agent's dock with room for it, as a `FreightContract` with `IsAgentCourier` (no fee or deposit, outside the freight limit of 3). It is delivered from the destination's Freight tab within the freight deadline (20 + 10 minutes a jump); late or lost with the ship, the mission fails.
    - **Mining quota:** 60 of the tier's ore (Tilarium, Currian, Idailia, Barinium, Gostian; Gostian at tier 6), handed in to the agent.
    - **Convoy escort:** 10 s after the pilot enters the named system a W7b convoy of the tier appears 30 m away (`Trade.StartEscortConvoy`) and makes for a starlane; 45 s later pirate raiders (2 / 2 / 3 / 3 / 4 / 4) strike it (`Trade.SpawnConvoyRaiders`: they go for the hauler and the pilot, and the convoy fights back).
