It succeeds when the hauler jumps out and fails when it dies. A convoy lost to its lifetime or a restart comes back on the next arrival.
    - **Scan-down:** on arrival the agent places a lead with `Space.PlaceSignature` (`SignatureSiteRules.CreateAgentLead`: a combat site, cache site or derelict of the mission tier). Only the holder's party's probes find it (`SignatureSiteRules.CanScan`); it is outside the area's signature count and never respawns. Opening its site cache completes the mission for the holder in the opener's party.
    - **Data heist:** a lead data site held by a squad of the mission tier (`FactionAgents.HoldHeistSite`); one of its W12b data cans is the Target Data Can. Slicing it completes the mission for the holder in the slicer's party; a target destroyed by a failed slice passes the mark to another can, or a new site comes on the next arrival.
  - **Agent intel:** `Space.AgentIntelAvailable` is now true, and a data can's agent intel pick adds one `Player.AgentIntel` (no cap), which opens an intel lead on any agent's board. `slicing.py` still values the pick as a component in its credit model: the lead pays the agent rate plus tokens.
  - **Rewards** (`AgentMissionReward`): credits, standing with the agent's faction (+15 standard, +20 priority, +25 intel lead) and faction tokens.
    - **Credits:** the mission's time at 108% of its tier's space combat credits per hour (`scanning.combat_rate`, with lockboxes), less what the activity pays itself (kill loot, a site's cache, slicing, raider loot), plus the ore a quota gives up at 40%; never below 25% of the time's worth; rounded to 25. Time: 3 minutes plus 6 a jump to the system and back; kills 1 / 1.2 / 1.5 / 4 minutes (any, fighter, interceptor, gunship); 360 ore an hour; a courier 3 + 2 + 3 a jump. Examples at one jump: T1 kill 700, courier 575, mining 1,150, convoy 950, scan-down 650, heist 925; T3 1,500 / 1,225 / 2,550 / 2,025 / 1,350 / 1,900; T6 3,900 / 3,200 / 6,100 / 5,025 / 3,550 / 5,100.
    - **Payout** (`agents.py --sim`, every type equally, one jump): T1 4,371/h (107.8% of 4,055), T2 6,755 (107.7%), T3 9,193 (108.1%), T4 8,171 (108.1%), T5 10,119 (107.9%), T6 23,990 (108.0%); every faction's board mix 107.6-108.2%, and 107.5-108.2% at zero or two jumps. Tokens at their store value add about 16% (123-126%).
    - **Tokens** (decision: a currency, not an item, so they cannot be traded or sold): `CurrencyType` 5-10 (Republic Military, Czerka, Smuggler, Hutt Cartel, Mandalorian and Sith Military Tokens), shown in the Currencies window with the existing coin icons `iit_coin_001`-`006`. A mission pays 20% of its credits in tokens at 100 credits a token (at least 1; an intel lead twice that): T1 1-2, T3 2-5, T6 6-12.
  - **Token stores** (the agent's "Requisitions", `FactionAgentRules.GetStoreItems`, at both of a faction's agents): Ion, Incendiary and Seeker Missiles x10 (6 tokens, Neutral), the faction maker's Design Schematics (20, Trusted; Wild Space only designs left out), its manufacturer component with the maker's trait (25, Honored), the Rift Key (Tier 5.5) (20, Honored; tiers 1-5 come with W15c) and the maker's Advanced Ship Schematics (100, Exalted). Exchange values: a token buys about 100 credits of store goods (Design Schematic 2,000 credits, Ship Schematic 10,000, a manufacturer component twice a tier 5 Shield Projector's Cost, the Rift Key its Cost). Makers: Republic Military Republic, Czerka Czerka, Smugglers Corellian, Hutt Cartel Hutt, Mandalorian Mandalorian, Sith Military Sith.
  - **Tests:** `FactionAgentTests` (placement and the Module agents beside their Dock Masters, board rotation, slots and tiers, each mission type's terms, the runtime quests, kill, courier and lead checks, wiring, the payout against the reference, the 100-115% band, standing and intel gates, taken-offer pruning, the token currencies, the stores and their gates, persistence), `SpaceSlicingTests` (intel on), `TradeTests`, `TradeRiskTests`, `TradeStandingTests`, `ScanningTests`, `PlayerMessageAuditTests`.
  - **Not built here:** interior trophies at the stores (new `StructureType` entries), Ship Schematics for makers without an Advanced ship, a mission timer other than the courier's deadline, engine tests, and client evidence: the agents' spots, the dialog, the convoy escort's raiders against an NPC convoy, the leads and the heist site are unverified in a client.

## 30. Incursions

- **Schedule:** once a week, a Sith or Mandalorian armada invades one region (an orbit and its neighbors) for up to 48 hours.
- **What spawns:** waves, command capitals, and blockades at the region's docks. While a blockade holds, docking services there are offline.
- **Trade:** the region's prices spike while the incursion lasts.
- **Clearing it:** a region-wide reward, plus personal loot scaled by each player's tracked contribution.
- **Added by the incursions and Rift Runs update (W15b):**
  - **Numbers:** every incursion number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/events.py`, which generates `IncursionTuning` (`Service/SpaceService`) and the Bible's Events (Revamp) tab (`events_tab`, registered in `bible_tabs.py`). `events.py --sim` prints the payout against each tier's combat rate. Draft numbers, tune in testing.
  - **Schedule (decision):** a fixed weekly slot, Saturday 18:00 UTC, for up to 48 hours. `IncursionState` (a new entity, one record `SPACE_INCURSIONS`, no migration) saves the next start, the last region and the running incursion: a restart neither skips nor repeats a week, an incursion running at a restart resumes with its progress and contributions and its ships respawn, and a start the server missed by its whole window is skipped. The tick (`Space.ProcessIncursions`, 5 s) starts, advances and ends it. Only one runs at a time.
  - **Regions:** each of the 8 orbits with a dock and its starlane neighbours (`IncursionTuning.Regions`, from `trade.LANES`); never last week's region; the invader is the Sith Armada or the Mandalorian Armada at random. Each area fights at its own orbit tier (`TradeCatalog.Orbits`), so a tier 5 centre does not flood a tier 2 neighbour with tier 5 ships; the centre's tier sets the region reward.
  - **Spawns (decision, no new creatures):** the existing role ships of each tier and the faction capitals, renamed at spawn ("Sith Armada Fighter") and marked with the `INCURSION_ROLE` and `INCURSION_TIER` locals; they drop their normal loot. Every area holds 5 waves, one at a time (the W12a combat site squad of its tier, the next 60 s after the last is destroyed), then its command group: the command ship and two gunships. The tier 6 command ship is the Sith or Mandalorian capital itself (`sithcap3`, `mandocap1`, both `FACTION_CAPITAL_SCALE` 2.0); below tier 6 it is the tier's capital flying that capital's appearance, so the fight stays in its tier's band. Destroying the command group liberates the area.
  - **Blockades:** two gunships and a defense platform at every planet dock of the region (the `spc_dock` placeables with `CONVERSATION=StarportDockDialog`; the Abandoned Station's ship-only dock and player station docks hold none). While one holds, `StarportDockDialog` refuses docking ("The Sith Armada blockades this dock. Docking services are offline until the blockade is broken.") and the exchanges of its planet are closed: the Dock Master's Trade Exchange entry, a city exchange that trades through it, and buying or selling in an open window (`Trade.TryGetTrade`). Ships already docked can still launch.
  - **Price spike:** `TradePricing.WithPriceMultiplier` scales a market's price factor; every exchange whose dock launches into a region area prices buying and selling at x1.25 (`Space.GetIncursionPriceMultiplier`, in `Trade` and the exchange window's quotes). The HoloNet board prices it too (W11b, below).
  - **Contribution:** the two ship damage paths call `RecordIncursionDamage`; a destroyed incursion ship's points (Normal 1, Elite 2.5, Boss 9: their fight length against a Normal) are split between the ships that damaged it by damage dealt, and the killing blow adds 10%. A module repair on another player's ship in an incursion area (`ApplyRepairEnmity`) counts 0.5 points per tier fighter's hull + shield restored, but only damage incursion ships dealt that ship counts, each point once (`IncursionRules.CreditableRepair` against a per-ship ledger), so repairing damage from players, hazards or a repeated repair earns nothing. A ship's points are shared evenly by its pilot and its station crew. Points are kept by the tier of the area they were earned in.
  - **Payout:** when every area is liberated and every blockade broken, each contributor is paid per tier: credits per point (T1 9.1 to T6 58.9, points paid up to 600) and a pull from the tier's `SPACE_ELITE` table per 20 points (at most 20). With 5 points or more they also get the region reward: 0.35 h of the region tier's combat rate (1,420 to 7,770 credits; 0.4 h until W11e), 2 more pulls and a 10% chance at a Ship Schematic of the invader's maker. Items go to the ship hold, or the inventory on the ground. Players offline at the clear are paid at their next login (`IncursionState.PendingPayouts`). An incursion that runs out of time pays nothing more.
  - **Balance** (`events.py --sim`; the kills' own loot counts, as for exploring): an active participant fighting 4 hours of an incursion at the baseline 60 kills an hour earns 130% of their tier's combat rate at every tier (T1 5,270 an hour against 4,055; T5 11,908 against 9,161); 2 active hours 139-140% (140-141% before W11e), 8 hours 125%. Fights (`checks.py` section O): a wave needs 2 matched Fighters at T1-T4 (97-100%) and 3 at T5 (100%); a blockade 2 Fighters (100%); a command group about 6 (83-96%; 5 lose): group content, as an invasion should be. These are all-at-once fights; in the game ships engage as they notice a pilot. Tier 6 is above the simulator's ranks.
  - **DM tools and status:** the Space tab's Start Incursion button starts one centred on the DM's orbit by a random armada. `/incursion` shows the region, each area's stage, the blockades and the player's contribution, or when the next one is due. The Ship HUD's SHIP section has an Incursion line in an incursion area. Server-wide messages announce the start, each command group, broken blockades, liberated areas, the clear and a withdrawal.
  - **Tests:** `IncursionTests` (schedule and persistence, regions and selection, spawns, blockades and closed exchanges, the price spike, contribution, the payout, clearing, wiring).
  - **Added by the follow-ups update (W11b):** the HoloNet board prices the spike (`HoloNetRules.Build` takes each exchange's multiplier, `Space.GetIncursionPriceMultiplier`) and is rebuilt at once when an incursion starts or ends (`HoloNetRules.GetPriceKey`), otherwise it stays an hour stale. A closed exchange neither offers nor takes freight: accepting and delivering a contract there are refused with the blockade message before any deposit is taken (`Trade.IsFreightExchangeClosed`); a load waits for the blockade to fall and its deadline still runs. Tests: `IncursionTests`.
  - **Not built here:** player-station docks and exchanges are not blockaded (W11o built them, below); client verification of the waves, blockades, docking refusal, prices and payout. Incursion-specific creatures and capital battle events were built in W11o (section 16).
  - **Added by the station blockades update (W11o):** every player station standing in the region when an incursion starts is blockaded too: the same two gunships and defense platform at its anchor (the docking point's teleport waypoint), keyed like a planet dock (`IncursionRules.StationDockKey`) and saved in `ActiveIncursion.StationBlockades` (empty on older saves). Its `Blockades` entry serves no planet, so a station blockade closes only that station: docking at it is refused with the dock message, its Starport's Trade Exchange and freight are closed (`Space.GetIncursionExchangeRefusal(TradeDock)`), and a Jump Beacon will not jump a ship in (`JumpRefusal.DestinationBlockaded` 10; ships inside still launch and jump out). Breaking it broadcasts the station's name; the region still needs it broken to clear. A station retrieved or lost meanwhile has its blockade lifted silently within a minute (`ReleaseLostStationBlockades`). Stations founded mid-incursion are not blockaded; Wild Space stations never are (no incursion reaches Wild Space). Station raids, defenses and hostile boarding stay out of scope. Tests: `IncursionTests`. No client evidence.

## 31. Rift Runs

- **Rift Keys:** tiers 1 through 5.5, dropped by aces, Wild Space and salvage, and sold at faction stores.
- **The run:** a key opens a private pocket for 1-3 ships, built as new template areas with 3 rooms. It has a 15-minute timer, and the rooms escalate.
- **Finishing in time** pays loot scaled by the key's tier and the time left.
- **Running out of time** ejects everyone in escape pods, with the normal XP debt.
- **Added by the incursions and Rift Runs update (W15b):**
  - **Numbers:** every Rift Run number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/events.py`, which generates `RiftRunTuning` (`Service/SpaceService`), the six key blueprints and the Events (Revamp) tab's Rift Run tables.
  - **Keys:** `rift_key_t1`-`rift_key_t5` (new) and `rift_key_t55` (W12c), "Rift Key (Tier N)", miscellaneous small items on six existing keycard models (187, 196, 239, 240, 171 and W12c's 197) with an Activate Item property; costs from the run's premium over combat at the 40% markdown (550, 850, 1,150, 1,050, 1,250; 5.5 keeps 2,000). `RiftKeyItemDefinition` opens the pocket. **Sources:** each ace's own table drops its tier's key (weight 15, `rewards.ACE_LOOT_WEIGHTS`; tier 6 aces the 5.5 key); the W12c Wild Space tables keep the 5.5 key; an elite or boss wreck's rare find is its tier's key 5% or 10% of the time after every other share (`RiftRunTuning.SalvageKeyPercent`, 5.5 in Wild Space and for tier 6); a finished run has a 10% chance at the next tier's key; and the W15a faction token stores sell tier 1 up to the faction's highest agent dock tier (at most 5) beside the 5.5 key (`agents.py` `rift_keys`, `FactionAgentTuning.StoreRiftKeys`): tokens 6, 9, 12, 11, 13 and 20, standing Neutral, Trusted, Trusted, Honored, Honored and Honored. Republic Military and the Mandalorian and Sith stores sell tiers 1-5, Smugglers and the Hutt Cartel 1-4, Czerka 1-3.
  - **The pocket (decision):** one template area, `space_riftpocket` "Rift Pocket" (Module `are`/`git`/`gic`, copied from Wild Space's The Maw with its signatures, environment, spawn tables and station anchor removed; only `SPACE` = 1; listed in `module.ifo` and `Planet`'s space areas; needs a module repack), with three chambers at the `RIFT_ROOM_1`-`3` waypoints, at least 100 m apart. Each run is an instance (`Area.CreateInstance`), destroyed once empty; the template is removed from the area cache so nothing is ever placed in it. At most 10 pockets at once.
  - **Opening:** in space, piloting, out of combat and decloaked, the key opens a pocket for the user's ship and the nearest party ships within 30 m, 3 ships in all (`RiftRunRules.PickParty`); their crews come aboard with them. The key is spent when the pocket opens.
  - **Chambers:** the Outer Drift (two waves of a fighter and an interceptor), the Shear Zone (a sniper and an electronic warfare ship, then a fighter and a bomber) and the Rift Heart (the tier's defense platform). The tier 5.5 Rift Raiders beat a matched pilot two at a time, so their chambers send them one at a time (4, 4 and 2 waves; their heart is a gunship, then its logistics ship, since they fly no platform). Each extra ship adds one ship to every wave of a chamber. Waves come one at a time, 5 s apart; a cleared chamber pulls the party into the next after 8 s. Ships are the tier's role ships (`t1`-`t5`) or the Rift Raiders (5.5) and drop their normal loot. `RiftRunRules.Step` holds the sequence.
  - **Timer and payout:** 15 minutes, with warnings at 5 minutes, 1 minute, 30 s and 10 s and a Rift Run line on the Ship HUD. Clearing the Rift Heart in time pays each ship the key's base credits (130 to 400) x (0.75 + the fraction of the timer left), shared evenly by its pilot and station crew, plus 2 pulls from the tier's elite table (`SPACE_ELITE_WS` at 5.5) into the pilot's hold, 3 with a third of the timer left; the party is returned where it opened the pocket 15 s later.
  - **Time out:** the pocket collapses and destroys every ship in it (`EffectDeath` on each pilot), so everyone aboard ejects in escape pods to their dock with the normal XP debt, cargo spill and repair bill (section 9, W2d). A ship whose pilot logged out in a pocket is sent back to its dock when the pocket closes (no debt, no payout). A restart empties every pocket: ships return to their docks at boot (their current position is wiped, as for every ship).
  - **Balance** (`events.py --sim`, the solo run at the baseline kill pace; kills' loot counts): 10.5 kill equivalents in about 11 minutes with 27% of the timer left pays 130% of the tier's combat rate per minute of run at every tier (T3 2,030 credits of value for 11 minutes; 5.5 2,498 for 10.5). Finishing with no time left pays about 90%, with half the timer left about 205%. Fights (`checks.py` section O): every solo wave is a pair, one ship (5.5) or one elite the matched Fighter wins (97-100%), and the party-scaled waves win at 100% with 2 and 3 ships; a solo run's fight time is 2.7 minutes at tier 1, 6.5-6.8 at tiers 3-5 and 8.2 at 5.5, inside the timer with the delays and shield regeneration between waves.
  - **Tests:** `RiftRunTests` (keys and their sources, the pocket template, the party, chamber scaling and sequence, the timer and collapse, the payout), `SpaceAceTests`, `WildSpaceTests` and `FactionAgentTests` updated.
  - **Not built here:** rejoining a run after logging out (built in W11k); a DM tool for pockets (built in W11n); a template per tier with its own chamber layout (built in W11o, section 16); client verification of the pocket, the chambers, the timer and the collapse.

## 32. Cloaking

- **Cloaking Device:** Mid slot, Piloting. It fits Small hulls and Blockade Runners only.
  - Interceptors, Bombers and Explorers get a role bonus: a faster cloak and a smaller speed penalty.
  - Blockade Runners can cloak with cargo aboard.
- **While cloaked:**
  - The ship is hidden from other players.
  - It can't use modules or abilities, and its toggled modules switch off.
  - It moves slower.
- **Decloaking:** locks firing for 5 seconds.
- **Counters:** a Sensor Sweep, or any ship within 10 m, reveals a cloaked ship. The observing ship's Espionage rank stretches that range by up to half (W11n).
- **Replaces** today's blanket stealth block in space.
- **Added by the cloaking update (W16):**
  - **Numbers:** every cloaking number is in `tools/SpaceRevampBalance/cloak.py`, which generates `CloakTuning` (`Service/SpaceService`) and the Bible's Cloaking (Revamp) tab (`bible_tabs.py`; the Ship Modules (Revamp) tab's Cloaking Device row and reveal-range column read it too). Draft numbers, tune in testing.
  - **Cloaking Device:** family 45 (`ShipModuleFamily.CloakingDevice`, `ShipModuleType.CloakingDevice` 26), `sm_cloak_00`-`sm_cloak_45` "Cloaking Device Mk I-X" (`catalog.py` `CLOAK_LATE_FAMILIES`): Mid, Piloting (rank N needs rank N, so Mk I fits any pilot), toggle, 1 s cycle, 2 capacitor, no range. Following the Ship Modules (Revamp) tab's draft, only the reveal range changes with the rank: 10 - rank / 9 m (Mk I-II 10 m, Mk X 5 m). Recipes are `RecipeType` 7533-7542, numbered after the Data Analyzer's 7532, each with a Sensor Suite of its tier (the W9a mapping) and the tier's refined ore. Art: the item reuses ESS9 inventory model 36 (all of ESS9's 1-254 are taken, so only the action icon is new, `iit_smcloak` with its cooldown frames; source `cloaking_device.svg`/`.png` in `SWLOR_Haks/sw_item_source/general`).
  - **Fit:** `ShipFitRules.GetFitError` calls `ShipCloakRules.CanFit`: every Small hull and the Blockade Runner (Pirate Freighter, Barracuda), nothing else. The Ship Management window shows "A Cloaking Device fits only Small hulls and Blockade Runners."
  - **Phases** (`ShipCloakBook`, in memory, gone with the flight): pressing the device starts the **cloak time** (4 s; 2 s for Interceptors, Bombers and Explorers; 2 s on the Pirate Freighter), during which the ship is still seen. Then it is **cloaked**. From the press the ship slows to 50% speed (65% on the role-bonus hulls; `ApplyShipSpeed`, the Ship HUD's Speed and power routing multiply with it), every other toggle switches off with the new auto-off reason `ShipToggleAutoOffReason.Cloaked`, no other module can be used (`ShipCloakRules.GetModuleBlock`; only the device itself answers, to switch it off) and no ability can (`Ability.cs`: "You cannot use abilities while your ship is cloaked"); the Scanner tab's Sensor Sweep and Jump to Signature are refused the same way.
  - **Cargo:** every cycle the device re-validates through `Space.CloakError`. Only a Blockade Runner may carry cargo (commodities or sealed freight, `Trade.GetUsedVolume`); any other hull is refused at the press and a cloak ends ("Cloaking Device switched off: Only Blockade Runners can cloak with cargo aboard.") within a second of cargo arriving.
  - **Hidden from players:** the W8a mechanism. When the cloak time ends the ship gets a global `VisibilityType.Hidden` override and a per-player `Visible` override for the pilot, the pilot's party and crew, DMs, every player within the reveal range and every player a Sensor Sweep revealed it to. A one-second tick (`Space.ProcessCloaks`) moves those per-player overrides as ships come and go, and a ghost effect shows the pilot their own cloak. Leaving the cloak, space, the area, or dying restores everything.
  - **Reveal:** any ship within the device's reveal range sees the ship, players and NPCs alike (the Barracuda's signature shortens it to 5 m). A **Sensor Sweep** fills W12a's `Space.IsShipCloaked` hook: the sweep lists a cloaked ship and `RevealCloakedShip` shows it to the sweeper and their party for 10 s, so it also lists as hostile when it is.
  - **NPC aggro (decision):** `Stealth.IsHiddenFrom` and `Stealth.CanAcquireAggro` ask `Space.IsShipHiddenFrom`, so every NPC AI path that skips an invisible or stealthed player skips a cloaked ship: an NPC keeps its enmity on the ship but no longer sees it as a target, gains no new aggro from the aggro aura, its toggled weapons switch off (their target is "gone") and the space NPC tick picks the next visible enmity target (`GetHighestEnmityAttackTarget`). Completing the cloak and decloaking both make enemies choose again, so a decloaked ship is attacked again at once. An NPC ship within the reveal range still sees it, as the player would. Wild Space's Rift Raiders and every other preset NPC ship run the same space NPC tick, so they drop a cloaked target the same way. The same hidden check removes the ship from `IsShipEnemy` (area weapons, mines, auras), from a player's selected target and from `GetSelectedTarget`.
  - **Decloaking:** pressing the device again, running out of capacitor, cargo, a jump, docking, a logout or dying ends the cloak; a Sensor Sweep never does. Damage does too: a ship still cloaking loses the cloak and nothing locks; a ship that had vanished decloaks. A decloak after vanishing locks every weapon and weapon ability for 5 s ("Your weapons are locked for N more seconds after decloaking"): the module gate, `FireStep` and `SpaceAbilities.GetFireError`. Other modules are not locked.
  - **Pirate ambushes (decision):** a cloaked ship slips past the pirate ambush roll (`Space.DodgesPirateAmbush`, checked before the roll in `Trade.RollPirateAmbush`; a ship still cloaking is rolled, and raiders already on it lose it as above). A **customs scan is not dodged**: a patrol reads transponders, not hulls, and the 30 s answer, Slice the Scan and the standing rules work as before, so the Blockade Runner's cloak does not undo the contraband balance.
  - **Replaced stealth block:** `Space.PreventSpaceStealth` ("You cannot enter stealth mode in space.") is gone. `Space.RedirectSpaceStealth` handles the same event: native stealth still never hides a ship (the action mode is cleared), but with a Cloaking Device fitted the stealth control presses it, and without one the pilot is told "Only a Cloaking Device hides a ship in space." `Stealth.GateStealthEntry` leaves space to it, so its "Stealth perk is required" refusal no longer shows over the pilot's ship. Ground stealth is unchanged.
  - **Ship HUD:** the Stats tab's SHIP section gets a Cloak line while a device is fitted (Off, Cloaking (N s), Cloaked, Weapons locked (N s)) and shows the cloak-adjusted speed.
  - **Tests:** `CloakingTests` (catalog, recipes, fit, cargo, role bonus and hull signatures, phases, what a cloaked ship cannot do, the decloak lock, both reveal paths, NPC and ambush wiring, the stealth block's replacement, the HUD), plus the updated icon, message audit and Bible tests; no engine test was added.
  - **Bible:** the Ship Modules (Revamp) tab's Cloaking Device row and reveal-range column and the new Cloaking (Revamp) tab are written by `bible_tabs.py`, and the Engineering Recipes rows for `sm_cloak_*` by `bible_recipes.py`; all are in the workbook.
  - **Not built here:** the Espionage bonus to the reveal range (built in W11n, section 16: +1% per rank of the observing ship's best Espionage, up to +50%), the Sith Infiltrator's longer Sensor Sweep, an Afterburner, Interdictor and Ion Web interaction with a cloak (built in W11k), DM tools for a cloak, and client verification of the vanishing, the ghost effect, the reveal and the HUD line.

## Appendix A: Perk drafts

Draft rows for the Bible. Where a ground perk does the same job, its numbers are copied and the **Source** column names it. "New" rows have no ground match: their numbers are a starting point for the combat simulator. Everything here needs owner approval before code (section 17).

**Shared rules for every space style**

- **Ported, not invented:** every row ports an existing ground perk (Source column) with its numbers, durations, STM and cooldown. Only the targeting changes: abilities fire your ship's weapons, and allies are party ships. Unlock ranks follow the Piloting schedule, so a row can unlock at a different rank than its source. A row marked **New** has no ground match.
- **Durations:** buffs and debuffs last 30 s and capstones 45 s, as on the ground.
- **Activation:** every space ability has a 0 s activation delay, because movement cancels a cast (`UsePerkFeat.cs`). No channels.
- **One impact:** an ability fires one weapon unless it says otherwise, so its damage never scales with how many weapons a ship carries. **Weapon DMG** is the DMG of the fitted weapon it fires.
- **Areas:** "near your ship" and "near the target" use the source's radius scaled to space distances; radii are drafted in the Bible (section 17). **Command range** is 30 m base (ground Leadership uses 5 m).
- **Allies:** party ships. Crew must be in the captain's party, so they count.
- **Threat follows the ground enmity rules** (`Service/Enmity.cs`):
  - Space damage goes through `Enmity.CalculateDamageEnmity`, so `DamageEnmityPercentAdjustment` (the tank stance's +100%) applies. Today modules call `ModifyEnmity` with raw damage and skip it.
  - Repairs generate heal enmity like ground healing: half the repair, to enemies holding the repaired ship. Today repairs generate none.
  - Goads use `Enmity.TryTaunt`: threat catches up to the highest visible rival plus a bonus, once, with no target lock.
  - Flat bursts use `ModifyEnmity`. Percent modifiers (stances, statuses) stack to the existing +50% clamp.
- **Statuses:** ground statuses apply to ships through the shared status system: Burn, Blind, Dazed, Exposed, Disarming Shot, Weakened and fragmentation. The hard crowd-control rules apply to Dazed.
- **Stats:** the space formulas must read the existing Attack, Accuracy, Defense and Evasion percent stats, which `Space.cs` ignores today.
- **Where perks apply:** a perk works wherever its condition is met, on any hull and from any station (cross-skill rule in `SkillPerkStandards.md`).

## Appendix D: Components and sourcing

Every ship recipe uses its generic components, its role's component, and its manufacturer's component. Advanced ships add one rare component. Quantities and drop chances are drafted in the Bible.

### Generic components

| Component | Crafted by | Main inputs | Used by ships | Also from |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Hull Frame | Smithery | Refined ore, metals; Durelium in the T1-2 band | Every ship | Salvage |
| Ship Reactor | Engineering | Electronics, refined ore; Tibanna Gas in the T3-4 band | Every ship | Salvage |
| Thruster Assembly | Smithery | Refined ore, fiberplast | Every ship (two for Interceptors and Blockade Runners) | Salvage |
| Energy Emitter | Engineering | Electronics, crystals | Fighters, Frigates, Cruisers | Salvage |
| Ordnance Assembly | Smithery | Refined ore, metals | Bombers | Salvage |
| Shield Projector | Engineering | Electronics, crystals | Gunships, Logistics | Salvage |
| Sensor Suite | Engineering | Electronics, gems | Interceptors, Explorers, Command Ships | Salvage, data sites |
| Extraction Head | Engineering | Ground refined ore (Veldite ... Jasioclase), electronics | Mining Barges, Industrials | Salvage |
| Life Support System | Agriculture | Plants and food goods, fiberplast | Light and Heavy Freighters, Blockade Runners, Cruisers, Command Ships | None |

### Manufacturer components

These are not crafted, and they're tradeable. Each one is easiest to get from its manufacturer's side of the galaxy, so flying a maker's ships pulls you toward that faction. Every one also drops, more slowly, from data sites and aces, so no character needs standing with a faction to build its ships.

| Component | Manufacturer | Sources |
|---|---|---|
| Republic Shield Matrix | Republic | Republic Military agent store; Republic freight contracts; data sites and aces (slower) |
| Sith Targeting Core | Sith | Sith Military agent store; Imperial (T6) ships and Sith capitals; their wrecks; data sites and aces (slower) |
| Mandalorian Plating | Mandalorian | Mandalorian agent store; Clan Zel Arun (T4) ships and Mandalorian capitals; their wrecks; data sites and aces (slower) |
| Czerka Industrial Frame | Czerka | Czerka agent store; Czerka freight contracts; rare output from Assembly Line holdings; data sites and aces (slower) |
| Hutt Cargo Coupling | Hutt | Hutt Cartel agent store; Hutt enforcers; their wrecks; data sites and aces (slower) |
| Corellian Drive Kit | Corellian | Smugglers agent store; pirate raiders and the T1-T5 pirate factions; their wrecks; data sites and aces (slower) |
| Chiss Sensor Array | Chiss | Data sites, aces, and the black markets at Smuggler's Moon, Tatooine and Hutlar |
| Raider Refit Hull | Rift Raider | Wild Space only: Rift Raider ships and cache sites; Wild Space data sites and aces (slower) |

### Rare components (Advanced ships)

| Component | Crafted by | Inputs | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Thorilide Core | Engineering | Thorilide (capital-ship kills) + Ship Reactor | Advanced Republic, Sith, Corellian and Chiss ships |
| Aurodium Plating | Smithery | Aurodium (Korriban Debris Field, Wild Space) + Hull Frame | Advanced Mandalorian, Hutt and Czerka ships |
| Prototype Core | Engineering | Prototype tech (Wild Space) + Sensor Suite | Rift Raider ships |

A salvaged component carrying a rare trait can stand in for the rare component in any Advanced recipe. That makes elite and boss wrecks worth salvaging.

### Schematics

| Unlock | Sources |
|---|---|
| Ship Schematic (Advanced ship recipe) | That manufacturer's agent store at high standing, aces, incursion rewards; Chiss from data sites; Rift Raider from Wild Space |
| Design Schematic (one ship's skin) | Aces, bosses, rare salvage, Rift Runs, faction stores |

### Loot table changes

- **Faction NPC ships** drop their manufacturer's component: a low chance on basic ships, higher on elites and bosses. Map each NPC faction to the manufacturer its ships resemble while drafting.
- **Wrecks** (Salvager) yield Ship Scrap, salvaged generic components, the wreck's manufacturer component, and, on elites and bosses, rare-trait parts.
- **Aces** drop Ship Schematics, Design Schematics and rare components.
- **NPC ships** sometimes drop space lockboxes (section 12), at every tier.
- **Wild Space** drops prototype tech, Raider Refit Hulls and Thorilide.
- **Incursions** reward Sith or Mandalorian Ship Schematics, matching the invader.
- **Rift Runs** drop rare-trait parts and Design Schematics.
- **Registration:** every new item source is registered for `EconomyObtainabilityCoverageTests` (`EconomyRestrictedItems.md`).
